Eurodollar stock, break-adjusted annual growth rates (LBS): cross-border USD claims of banks outside the US: 2024-Q4 +7.8%, 2025-Q1 +4.7%, Q2 +7.3%, Q3 +8.5%, Q4 +7.8%, 2026-Q1 +11.9%. Liabilities: 2025-Q1 +3.1%, Q2 +6.7%, Q3 +5.6%, Q4 +6.8%, 2026-Q1 +9.4%. This is the cleanest growth series in the whole channel — BIS calculates it net of exchange-rate valuation and net of reporting breaks — and it is accelerating sharply. The last time claims growth ran near 12% was pre-GFC.

Turnover: FX swaps +6% April 2022 → April 2025 (final data), against +42% spot, +51% outright forwards, +108% options. The funding instrument stagnated; the hedging instruments exploded. Turnover of FX swaps' share of the market fell from 51% to 42%, the lowest since 2010.

## Series

| Series | Publisher | Freq | Cost | Gives | Limitations |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BIS OTC derivatives statistics, FX risk category — notional amounts outstanding (dataflow WS_OTC_DERIV2, tables D5.1 / D6 / D9) | Bank for International Settlements | Semiannual (end-June, end-December) | free | The headline. USD-leg notional of FX swaps + outright forwards + currency swaps, net-net, with counterparty-sector, instrument, currency and (all-currency only) maturity cuts. 14-dimension SDMX key: FREQ.DER_TYPE.DER_INSTR.DER_RISK.DER_REP_CTY.DER_SECTOR_CPY.DER_CPC.DER_SECTOR_UDL.DER_CURR_LEG1.DER_CURR_LEG2.DER_ISSUE_MAT.DER_RATING.DER_EX_METHOD.DER_BASIS | No counterparty-country dimension (DER_CPC is always 5J). No reporting-jurisdiction dimension (DER_REP_CTY always 5J) — verified, so the coverage break cannot be decomposed. FX swaps cannot be separated from outright forwards (instrument codes E, H, G, J return no data for outstandings — verified). NDFs not separately identified. Currency breakdown limited to seven currencies. Triennial-year level breaks. Not netted for bilateral master-agreement netting. |
| BIS Triennial Central Bank Survey — OTC FX turnover, April 2025 (dataflow WS_DER_OTC_TOV; tables D11.1–D11.6) | Bank for International Settlements | Triennial (April of survey year) | free | The only source that splits FX swaps from outright forwards, and the only maturity detail for FX swaps (overnight / ≤7d / ≤1m / ≤3m / ≤6m / >6m). 52 jurisdictions, 1,100+ dealers. Also the counterparty decomposition inside 'other financial institutions' (non-reporting banks 24% of turnover, institutional investors 13%, hedge funds and PTFs 8%, official sector 3%) and the market-facing / non-market-facing split. | Turnover, not stocks — it is rollover, not credit. One month, and April 2025 was distorted by the tariff announcements (BIS estimates $1.5tn/day of the $9.5tn was event-driven). Preliminary-to-final revisions are material: total revised $9.6tn → $9.5tn, outright forwards growth revised +60% → +51%. Sales-desk location, not trading-desk location. |
| BIS locational banking statistics, USD positions, residence basis, foreign-currency view (dataflow WS_LBS_D_PUB) | Bank for International Settlements | Quarterly | free | The eurodollar system measured directly. Setting L_CURR_TYPE=F (foreign currency) with L_DENOM=USD automatically excludes US-located offices, so the aggregate 5A IS 'banks outside the United States'. Stocks (L_MEASURE=S), BIS-calculated FX-and-break-adjusted quarterly flows (L_MEASURE=F) and annual growth (L_MEASURE=G). 12-dimension key: FREQ.L_MEASURE.L_POSITION.L_INSTR.L_DENOM.L_CURR_TYPE.L_PARENT_CTY.L_REP_BANK_TYPE.L_REP_CTY.L_CP_SECTOR.L_CP_COUNTRY.L_POS_TYPE | In the foreign-currency residence view ONLY the total is published: counterparty sector and counterparty country both collapse to 'A'/'5J' (verified — sector and country cuts return a single total). Several reporting countries return NaN for USD by confidentiality, including Singapore, Indonesia, Saudi Arabia and Malaysia — Singapore is the world's third-largest FX centre. Frequent OBS_STATUS='B' break flags on the stock series: never difference the stocks, use L_MEASURE=F. Local (in-country) USD liabilities are not retrievable in this cut. |
| BIS locational banking statistics by nationality — USD liabilities by parent country (same dataflow, L_CURR_TYPE=A) | Bank for International Settlements | Quarterly | free | USD liabilities by nationality of the parent bank, with a counterparty-sector cut (including L_CP_SECTOR=I 'banks, related offices' so intragroup can be stripped out). This is the cut that supports 'non-US banks' dollar debt' and the Aldasoro et al residual method. At 2026-Q1: all parents $19.52tn, US parents $4.46tn, related offices $6.21tn. | Mirror image of the residence view: when parent nationality is used, reporting country collapses to 5A — so you can have nationality OR location, never both, in the public data. Only 14 parent nationalities are published and they sum well short of the total, leaving a large unallocated residual. Cross-border only; local positions are not in this cut. |
| BIS Global Liquidity Indicators — US dollar credit to non-bank borrowers outside the United States (dataflow WS_GLI) | Bank for International Settlements | Quarterly | free | The on-balance-sheet denominator against which the off-balance-sheet estimate is compared. This is exactly the series behind BMM's Graph 2.A. 8-dimension key: FREQ.CURR_DENOM.BORROWERS_CTY.BORROWERS_SECTOR.LENDERS_SECTOR.L_POS_TYPE.L_INSTR.UNIT_MEASURE. Country code 3P = 'all countries excluding residents' (i.e. non-US borrowers); sector N = non-banks; instrument B = credit, G = bank loans, D = debt securities. Set UNIT_MEASURE=771 for the BIS-calculated % y/y. | Only three currencies (USD, EUR, JPY). Combines LBS bank credit with debt-securities data compiled on a different basis. Country breakdown limited to a set of large EMEs plus regional aggregates. Definitionally on-balance-sheet only — by construction it cannot see the swap channel, which is the whole point. |
| OCC Quarterly Report on Bank Trading and Derivatives Activities (Call Report Schedule RC-L; holding companies FR Y-9C Schedule HC-L) | US Office of the Comptroller of the Currency | Quarterly | free | US insured banks' FX contract notional, needed for the US-bank subtraction in the non-US-bank estimate. Table 10 gives notional by underlying risk exposure; Table 15 gives the top-25 bank distribution with a foreign-exchange percentage column; Table 14 gives the parallel BHC view. Q1 2026: FX $75,318bn; Q4 2025 $57,266bn; Q1 2025 $55,854bn. Q2 2022 (the BMM reference date) $41,915bn. | SERIOUS AND CURRENT: total notional jumped 42.5% quarter-on-quarter at Q1 2026 to $296.5tn, FX +31.5%, with no break note anywhere in the report. The jump sits in Goldman Sachs Bank USA ($60.4tn) and Morgan Stanley Bank NA ($55.6tn), each of which now reports MORE derivatives notional at the insured-bank level than its own holding company reports at the group level in Table 14 (GS Group $50.9tn, Morgan Stanley $44.9tn). That is internally impossible. Do not use the Q1 2026 OCC FX figure without first reconciling bank-level RC-L against group-level HC-L. Separately, the OCC category 'FX' mixes forwards, swaps, currency swaps and options, so it does not map cleanly onto the BIS instrument codes. |
| Borio, McCauley and McGuire, 'Dollar debt in FX swaps and forwards: huge, missing and growing', BIS Quarterly Review, December 2022, pp 67–73, with Technical Annex | Bank for International Settlements | One-off | free | The estimator itself. The Technical Annex on p.73 is the operative text: Graph 2.B off-balance-sheet non-bank liabilities are 'one half of outstanding dollar FX swaps with non-banks', assuming non-banks inside the US are not dollar borrowers via these instruments; Graph 2.D is (a) one half of global outstanding USD FX swaps with all counterparties, less (b) one half of US banks' outstanding FX swaps from OCC data, plus (c) US banks' estimated net provision of USD via FX swaps derived from LBS and CBS. Footnote 4 supplies the justification for the one-half: Aldasoro et al (2020) show the global banking sector is close to balanced in dollar-leg FX swaps, so if dealers and customers are the whole market, non-bank customers must be balanced too, hence half of the customer notional is customers owing dollars. | Graph 2.B is exactly reproducible (I reproduce $26.05tn against their published $26tn). Graph 2.D is not — see build_recipe step 6. Vintage end-June 2022 throughout. |
| McGuire, 'FX swaps and forwards in global dollar debt: known knowns and known unknowns', Japan and the World Economy, vol 64, December 2022, article 101160 | Elsevier (author at BIS) | One-off | paid | The methodology paper the operator asked for: a systematic review of how well the four benchmark collections — BIS international banking statistics (LBS + CBS), BIS OTC derivatives statistics, IMF/national International Investment Position statistics, and the IMF Coordinated Portfolio Investment Survey — capture foreign-currency positions on and off balance sheet, where each breaks, and what modifications would close the gaps. | PAYWALLED — ScienceDirect returned 403 and no BIS working-paper version exists under that title. I could not read it and I am not going to paraphrase a paper I have not read. Free substitutes that cover most of the same ground and that I did read: McGuire, Santos, von Peter and Zhu, 'The currency dimension in international financial statistics', BIS QR June 2024 Box A (currency coverage by collection, Table A) at bis.org/publ/qtrpdf/r_qt2406b_box.pdf; and Avdjiev, McGuire and von Peter, 'International finance through the lens of BIS statistics: derivatives markets', BIS QR December 2025 pp 63–78, which is the current primer plus an online annex on scope and dimensions. |
| Kloks, McGuire, Ranaldo and Sushko, 'Bank positions in FX swaps: insights from CLS', BIS Quarterly Review, September 2023 — and its Online Statistical Annex | Bank for International Settlements / CLS Group | One-off (CLS data itself is daily, commercial) | confidential/regulatory | The only published route to the GEOGRAPHY the OTCD statistics cannot give. The annex sets out, step by step, how to estimate a banking system's on-balance-sheet net position in a currency ('currency gap') from the IBS: (a) net position in currency X booked by that system's offices located outside the X-issuing area; (b) minus the net position in non-X currencies booked by its offices inside the X-issuing area (an indirect measure used because book equity and non-deposit liability reporting is inconsistent across CBS reporters); (c) minus net local claims in local currency in countries that do not report the LBS, applied only to the home currency. The residual against CLS-settled FX swaps gives the implied non-CLS derivative position. | CLS transaction data is commercial and covers only CLS-settled pairs. The IBS gap estimate is explicitly indirect: China, India, Japan, Panama, Turkey, Singapore and the United States do not fully report local positions vis-à-vis residents for all parent nationalities. |
| Aldasoro, Ehlers, McGuire and von Peter, 'Global banks' dollar funding needs and central bank swap lines', BIS Bulletin no 27, 16 July 2020 | Bank for International Settlements | One-off | free | The residual method for getting a banking system's unobserved net FX-derivative position: infer it as the difference between total on-balance-sheet dollar assets and dollar liabilities, on the assumption that banks do not run significant open currency positions. Also the definitional basis for 'non-US banks' gross dollar liabilities' — USD liabilities raised in any of the 47 BIS reporting countries including the US, excluding intragroup but including liabilities to unaffiliated banks, with China and Russia from end-2015. Gives $13tn at end-2019, of which 78% booked outside the US. | China does not report its banks' USD positions vis-à-vis residents of China and most major reporting countries do not report Chinese banks' dollar positions, so Chinese banks are systematically understated. Intragroup positions are reported with error, which yields two equally valid estimates of the net interbank and cross-currency positions. |
| Comotto, 'Are FX swaps and forwards missing global debt? Why the BIS is wrong', ICMA Centre Blog, 22 September 2017 | ICMA Centre, Henley Business School, University of Reading | One-off | free | The standing critique, and it must be engaged rather than dismissed. Two separate arguments. (i) Taxonomic: BIS treats currency swaps as analogous to FX swaps; Comotto argues a correctly defined currency swap has no initial principal exchange (only periodic interest exchanges plus principal at maturity), so it is analogous to an outright forward, not to an FX swap; what the market usually calls a currency swap with an initial exchange is, on his reading, a currency swap combined with a spot deal. He explicitly says this is not fatal to the BIS argument. (ii) Substantive, and it is the one that bites: a repo borrower keeps the collateral's risk and return AND gets cash, so leverage rises; an FX swap borrower surrenders the purchasing power of the currency sold, so leverage does not rise. He also argues forwards are correctly off balance sheet because balance sheets measure current, not future, assets and liabilities, and that the right measure of the exposure is net replacement cost. | Written against the 2017 predecessor paper, whose estimate was $10.7tn, not the 2022 paper. A blog post, not peer-reviewed. It does not address the outright-forward-as-unfunded-leverage point, nor the possibility that the currency surrendered was itself borrowed. |
| Cross-currency basis (the observable price of the balance-sheet constraint) | No primary-source series exists | n/a | paid | NOTHING, FROM A PRIMARY SOURCE — this is a finding, not an omission. There is no BIS, Fed, ECB or IMF published cross-currency basis series. BIS Quarterly Review charts of the basis are sourced to Bloomberg. To construct it you need OIS curves (all free and primary: SOFR from the New York Fed, €STR from the ECB, TONA from the Bank of Japan, SONIA from the Bank of England) AND FX forward points, which no primary source publishes free — they require Bloomberg, LSEG or a dealer feed. Free primary observables of the same underlying constraint, all of which are currently quiet: Federal Reserve H.4.1 'Central bank liquidity swaps' (weekly, currently $123mn, i.e. test operations only); ECB and Bank of Japan USD liquidity-providing operation allotment results (published per operation); the Fed's FIMA repo facility usage. | Any basis series in a client deck is a vendor series. State the vendor. Do not present it as official data. |

## Build recipe

Everything below was executed live against the BIS SDMX API and reproduces the published Borio–McCauley–McGuire numbers before rolling them forward. Base URL for all BIS pulls: https://stats.bis.org/api/v2/data/dataflow/BIS/{DATAFLOW}/1.0/{KEY}?startPeriod=YYYY-MM-DD&format=csv

STEP 0 — LEARN THE KEY GRAMMAR. WS_OTC_DERIV2 has 14 dimensions in this order: FREQ . DER_TYPE . DER_INSTR . DER_RISK . DER_REP_CTY . DER_SECTOR_CPY . DER_CPC . DER_SECTOR_UDL . DER_CURR_LEG1 . DER_CURR_LEG2 . DER_ISSUE_MAT . DER_RATING . DER_EX_METHOD . DER_BASIS. Codes you need: FREQ=H; DER_TYPE=A (notional outstanding), D (gross market value); DER_INSTR A=all, C=forwards and swaps (this is FX swaps + outright forwards + currency swaps, the BMM aggregate), D=outright forwards and FX swaps combined, I=currency swaps, R=options; DER_RISK=B (foreign exchange); DER_REP_CTY=5J; DER_SECTOR_CPY A=all, B=reporting dealers, C=other financial institutions, U=non-financial customers, K=CCPs; DER_CPC=5J; DER_SECTOR_UDL=A; currency legs TO1=all or USD; DER_ISSUE_MAT A=all, U=≤1y, D=1–5y, F=>5y; DER_RATING=A; DER_EX_METHOD=3; DER_BASIS=C (net-net, i.e. adjusted for inter-dealer double-counting — use C, not A or B).

STEP 1 — PULL THE HEADLINE STOCK.
Key H.A.C.B.5J.A.5J.A.TO1.USD.A.A.3.C → USD-leg forwards and swaps, all counterparties. Returns 2025-S2 = 113,499,999 (USD mn) = $113.50tn. Sanity-check the method by reading off 2022-S1 = $85.5tn, which is BMM's published $85tn.
Companion keys: change DER_CURR_LEG2 to TO1 for all currencies ($129.30tn); DER_INSTR to D for outright forwards + FX swaps ($74.02tn USD leg); to I for currency swaps ($39.47tn USD leg); to R for options ($15.89tn USD leg, EXCLUDE from the debt aggregate); to A for the whole FX risk category ($148.65tn).

STEP 2 — SPLIT BY COUNTERPARTY, WHICH IS THE WHOLE ESTIMATOR.
Run the STEP 1 key four times varying DER_SECTOR_CPY across B, C, U, K. At 2025-S2: dealers (B) $41.7tn, other financial institutions (C) $60.2tn, non-financial customers (U) $11.6tn, CCPs (K) $7.7tn (a subset of C, do not add).
DEALERS' DOLLAR POSITIONS WITH CUSTOMERS = total(A) − dealers(B) = 113.5 − 41.7 = $71.8tn. This is BMM's "$52 trillion in outstanding dollar positions with customers" rolled forward: at 2022-S1 the same subtraction gives 85.5 − 33.4 = $52.1tn, matching their text.

STEP 3 — APPLY THE ONE-HALF RULE TO GET NON-BANK OFF-BALANCE-SHEET DOLLAR DEBT.
OFF-BS non-bank dollar debt = 0.5 × (dealers' dollar positions with customers).
2025-S2: 0.5 × 71.8 = $35.90tn. Validation: 2022-S1 gives 0.5 × 52.1 = $26.05tn against BMM's published $26tn — reproduces to one decimal.
Understand what you are assuming. The one-half comes from Aldasoro et al (2020): the global banking sector is close to balanced in dollar-leg FX swaps, and since dealers plus customers exhaust the market, customers must be balanced too, so half of the customer notional represents customers owing dollars. The second assumption, stated in BMM's Technical Annex, is that non-banks inside the United States are not dollar borrowers via these instruments — they are dollar LENDERS, hedging modest foreign-currency assets. If you doubt either assumption, the sensitivity is linear: a 45/55 split moves the estimate to $32.3tn / $39.5tn.

STEP 4 — BUILD THE ON-BALANCE-SHEET DENOMINATOR (GLI).
Dataflow WS_GLI, key Q.USD.3P.N.A.I.B.USD → US dollar credit to non-bank borrowers outside the United States. Returns 2026-Q1 = $14.74tn. Validate against BMM by reading 2022-Q2 = $13.30tn, which is their "$13tn". Decompose with L_INSTR=G and LENDERS_SECTOR=B for bank loans ($6.68tn) and L_INSTR=D for debt securities ($8.06tn). For the growth rate append UNIT_MEASURE=771 instead of USD.
Now form the headline ratio: 35.90 / 14.74 = 2.44x at end-2025 against 26.05 / 13.30 = 1.96x at mid-2022. Report the ratio, not the difference, and attach the STEP 8 caveat.

STEP 5 — BUILD THE EURODOLLAR STOCK AND FLOW (LBS). This is the part that actually measures creation.
Dataflow WS_LBS_D_PUB, 12 dimensions: FREQ . L_MEASURE . L_POSITION . L_INSTR . L_DENOM . L_CURR_TYPE . L_PARENT_CTY . L_REP_BANK_TYPE . L_REP_CTY . L_CP_SECTOR . L_CP_COUNTRY . L_POS_TYPE.
The trick that makes this work: set L_DENOM=USD AND L_CURR_TYPE=F. "Foreign currency" means foreign to the reporting bank's location, so USD-plus-F mechanically excludes US-located offices. The aggregate L_REP_CTY=5A therefore IS "all banks outside the United States". (If you set L_CURR_TYPE=A instead, you get the by-nationality view and reporting country collapses to 5A — you cannot have both.)
  Stock, claims:      Q.S.C.A.USD.F.5J.A.5A.A.5J.N → 2026-Q1 $17.86tn
  Stock, liabilities: Q.S.L.A.USD.F.5J.A.5A.A.5J.N → 2026-Q1 $14.11tn
  BREAK-AND-FX-ADJUSTED QUARTERLY FLOW: Q.F.C.A.USD.F.5J.A.5A.A.5J.N → 2026-Q1 +$721.6bn; sum the last four quarters for +$1.90tn.
  BIS-calculated annual growth: Q.G.C.A.USD.F.5J.A.5A.A.5J.N → 2026-Q1 +11.9%.
DO NOT difference the stock series to get a flow. It carries OBS_STATUS='B' break flags in most years and is unadjusted for dollar-index moves. Use L_MEASURE=F. This is the single most important measurement rule in the whole channel.
For the nationality view (needed for step 6): Q.S.L.A.USD.A.{PARENT}.A.5A.A.5J.N with PARENT ∈ {5J, US, JP, GB, FR, DE, CH, CA, CN, ES, NL, SE, IT, BE}. 2026-Q1: all $19.52tn, US $4.46tn, so non-US-headquartered = $15.06tn. Strip intragroup with L_CP_SECTOR=I ($6.21tn for all parents).

STEP 6 — THE NON-US BANK ESTIMATE, AND WHY YOU SHOULD NOT PUBLISH IT.
BMM Technical Annex Graph 2.D: (a) one half of global outstanding dollar FX swaps with all counterparties, minus (b) one half of US banks' outstanding FX swaps from OCC data, plus (c) US banks' estimated net provision of dollars via FX swaps derived from the LBS and CBS.
(a) is trivially reproducible: 0.5 × 113.5 = $56.75tn at end-2025 (0.5 × 85.5 = $42.75tn at mid-2022).
(b) and (c) are not. Test the arithmetic at the published date: BMM report $39tn, so (b) − (c) must equal $3.75tn. The OCC's published FX contract notional at Q2 2022 was $41.9tn, so 0.5 × (b) alone would be $20.96tn, implying (c) ≈ $17.2tn of US-bank net dollar provision via FX swaps — an order of magnitude above anything in Aldasoro et al (2020). Conclusion: "US banks' outstanding FX swaps" in the annex is a narrower line item than the OCC's headline FX category, and it is not identified. THE NON-US BANK NUMBER IS NOT REPRODUCIBLE FROM PUBLISHED DATA. Say so. Publish (a) = $56.75tn as "total banking-sector dollar payment obligations under the symmetry assumption" and stop there, or go to the CLS/IBS route below.
And in any case the OCC input is currently broken: Q1 2026 total notional jumped 42.5% quarter-on-quarter to $296.5tn with FX up 31.5% to $75.3tn, with no break note, and Goldman Sachs Bank USA and Morgan Stanley Bank NA now report more notional at the insured-bank level than their own holding companies report on the FR Y-9C. Reconcile RC-L against HC-L before using it.

STEP 7 — GET THE GEOGRAPHY (the thing BIS says it cannot give you).
The OTCD statistics have no counterparty-country dimension: DER_CPC only ever takes 5J, and DER_REP_CTY only ever takes 5J. I verified both. So geography must be inferred from the banking statistics, two published ways:
  (i) THE RESIDUAL METHOD (Aldasoro, Ehlers, McGuire, von Peter, BIS Bulletin 27, 2020). For a banking system, compute total on-balance-sheet dollar assets and dollar liabilities from the LBS/CBS; the gap is the implied net FX-derivative position, on the assumption that banks do not run large open currency positions. This is how you learn that Japanese and Canadian banks are net dollar BORROWERS via FX swaps while Australian and (since 2017) UK banks are net dollar LENDERS.
  (ii) THE CLS/IBS METHOD (Kloks, McGuire, Ranaldo, Sushko, BIS QR Sep 2023, Online Statistical Annex). Estimate the currency gap for system i in currency X as: net position in X booked by offices outside the X-issuing area, MINUS net position in non-X currencies booked by offices inside the X-issuing area (used indirectly because equity and non-deposit liability reporting is inconsistent across CBS reporters), MINUS net local claims in local currency in non-LBS-reporting countries (home currency only). Then compare with CLS-settled FX swap positions; the difference is the implied non-CLS position. CLS data is commercial.

STEP 8 — RUN THE COVERAGE-BREAK DIAGNOSTIC BEFORE QUOTING ANY GROWTH RATE.
The semiannual OTCD covers 65 dealers in 12 jurisdictions; the Triennial adds 30-plus jurisdictions, and between surveys the BIS interpolates the extra segment. Every survey year the series steps up. Pull the full USD-leg forwards-and-swaps history and compute the survey-year step and the following half-year give-back:
  2018-S2 → 2019-S1 +9.0%, then −6.2%
  2021-S2 → 2022-S1 +5.6%, then −2.8%
  2024-S2 → 2025-S1 +15.9%, then −0.4%
The 2025 step is roughly double either precedent and did not reverse. BIS reports that non-regular reporters were 13% of FX notional in June 2025 (11% of all-risk notional, against 9% in 2022) but publishes no decomposition of the level break, and DER_REP_CTY gives you no way to build one. Quote the underlying rate off the comparable inter-survey stretch instead: 2022-S1 → 2024-S2, $85.5tn → $98.3tn, ≈5.7% p.a.

STEP 9 — SEPARATE ROLLOVER FROM CREDIT.
Dataflow WS_DER_OTC_TOV, same 14-dimension grammar, FREQ=A, DER_TYPE=U (daily-average turnover). Key A.U.H.B.5J.A.5J.A.TO1.TO1.A.A.3.C returns FX swap turnover: April 2025 $4,015bn/day, of which ≤7 days (DER_ISSUE_MAT=G) $2,725bn = 68% and overnight (DER_ISSUE_MAT=3) $1,197bn = 30%. Add outright forwards at $1,750bn/day. Combined $5.76tn/day annualises at 252 business days to ~$1,453tn against an $83.3tn stock: 17.4 turns per year, implied average residual life about three weeks. Write this ratio down next to any turnover figure you quote. Also strip the 13% of turnover that the Triennial flags as non-market-facing (back-to-back plus compression trades).

STEP 10 — PRICE THE CONSTRAINT, HONESTLY. There is no free primary cross-currency basis series. Construct it only if you have a forward-points feed: basis = (FX-swap-implied USD rate) − (USD OIS), with OIS legs from SOFR (New York Fed), €STR (ECB), TONA (Bank of Japan), SONIA (Bank of England) — all free and primary — and forward points from Bloomberg or LSEG, which are not. Label the vendor. As free primary substitutes for the stress signal, track Federal Reserve H.4.1 line "Central bank liquidity swaps" (currently $123mn, i.e. test operations only — no strain), plus ECB and BoJ USD liquidity-providing operation allotment results, plus FIMA repo usage.

STEP 11 — CHECK THE VINTAGE EVERY TIME. As at 21 Aug 2026: OTC derivatives outstanding last updated 15 May 2026, carrying 2025-S2 (end-Dec 2025); the next release is due around November 2026 with 2026-S1. LBS and GLI last updated 31 Jul 2026, carrying 2026-Q1. Triennial final data and FX settlement data released 15 Jun 2026. Confirm on data.bis.org/bulkdownload, which prints the update date per topic. Also check for revisions: the 2025 Triennial total FX turnover was revised from $9.6tn preliminary (30 Sep 2025) to $9.5tn final (Dec 2025), and outright-forward growth from +60% to +51%.

## Double-counting risk

This channel overlaps with almost everything else in the project. Never add across the bases below.

WITHIN THE CHANNEL. The USD-leg figure ($113.50tn) and the all-currency figure ($129.30tn) are the same contracts counted differently — every contract with a dollar leg appears in both. Currency swaps ($46.00tn) are already inside the $129.30tn; the outright-forwards-plus-FX-swaps aggregate ($83.30tn) is the rest of it. 46.00 + 83.30 = 129.30 by construction. Options ($19.35tn) sit outside that aggregate and inside the $148.65tn FX total. Adding any two of these produces nonsense. Likewise CCPs ($7.7tn) are a subset of other financial institutions ($60.2tn), not an addition to them.

AGAINST REPO. FX swaps and repo are substitutes, explicitly so — Comotto notes that thin repo markets in several Asian jurisdictions reflect competition from the FX swap market, which offers established liquidity without securities collateralisation. A dealer that funds the dollar leg of a swap by repoing Treasuries generates one position in the OTCD statistics and another in the repo statistics against the same underlying dollar. Whichever channel you attribute it to, do not attribute it twice.

AGAINST THE EURODOLLAR STOCK — THE BIGGEST TRAP IN THIS CHANNEL. When a dealer creates the dollar leg of an FX swap as a new deposit, that deposit IS a cross-border USD liability in the LBS. The $14.11tn eurodollar liability stock and the swap-derived $35.90tn are not independent measurements of two different things; one is partly the balance-sheet footprint of the other. They are also on incompatible bases (LBS is a residence-based on-balance-sheet stock with a sector and counterparty structure; the swap figure is a derived half of a net-net notional with neither).

AGAINST THE GLI ON-BALANCE-SHEET NUMBER. This one is deliberately non-overlapping and that is the point of the comparison: GLI's $14.74tn is by construction on-balance-sheet only, so the $35.90tn adds to rather than duplicates it. But the sum $50.6tn is not a meaningful total — one term is a measured stock of loans and bonds, the other is half of a derivative notional under a symmetry assumption. Present them as a ratio (2.44x), which is how BMM present them, never as a sum.

AGAINST INTRAGROUP. $6.21tn of the $19.52tn of cross-border USD bank liabilities is to related offices. Internal capital markets are growing faster than external inter-dealer activity — BIS reports intragroup FX turnover growing faster than total inter-dealer turnover across all instruments in 2025 — so this wedge is widening. Any "system-wide dollar debt" figure that does not strip it is counting the same dollar as it moves between a bank's own branches.

AGAINST TURNOVER-BASED CLAIMS ANYWHERE. 13% of April 2025 FX turnover is non-market-facing: back-to-back trades that shift risk between a dealer's own sales desks, and compression trades that exist to reduce notional. Neither is an economic transaction with an outside party.

AGAINST CARRY-TRADE AND PORTFOLIO-FLOW CHANNELS. The same yen-funded position appears as a yen loan in the LBS and GLI, as a yen-leg FX swap in the OTCD statistics, and as a portfolio inflow in the TIC data. BIS shows the linkage explicitly: yen loans to non-banks outside Japan grew nearly 75% between early 2022 and 2024 while yen-leg FX swaps rose in parallel, and other-financial-institution FX swap positions co-move with foreign purchases of US long-term debt securities (their estimated beta is 0.34, significant at 1%). It is one trade with three statistical shadows.

AGAINST THE INTEREST-RATE-DERIVATIVE CHANNEL. Currency swaps carry an interest-rate leg. The $846tn OTC derivatives total already contains the $148.65tn FX category; do not present FX derivatives as an addition to it.

AND THE CATEGORY ERROR THAT MATTERS MOST HERE: a notional is not a debt. In FX derivatives the notional does correspond to a real principal payment — BIS says so explicitly, and that is exactly what makes FX different from interest rate derivatives — but the gross market value of the entire FX risk category is a tiny fraction of the notional, and the payment obligation is offset by an equal and opposite receipt in the other currency. The $113.5tn is a gross settlement exposure, not net indebtedness and not money.

## What cannot be measured

Stated plainly, because several of these are permanent.

1. FX SWAPS CANNOT BE SEPARATED FROM OUTRIGHT FORWARDS IN THE STOCK DATA. Instrument codes E (outright forwards), H (FX swaps), G (NDFs) and J (deliverable forwards) return no data at all in the semiannual outstandings — I queried each and confirmed. Only the combined code D exists. This is not a nuance: an FX swap is a funding transaction with principal moving today, an outright forward is an unfunded hedge with nothing moving until maturity. They have opposite implications for whether purchasing power is being created. The split exists only in Triennial turnover, once every three years, for one month.

2. THERE IS NO GEOGRAPHY. DER_CPC (counterparty country) and DER_REP_CTY (reporting country) both take only the value 5J in the FX outstandings. BMM's own footnote 5 concedes it. The consequence is the one they emphasise: central bank swap lines in a crisis are sized without knowing who owes the dollars or where. The Dec 2025 BIS primer repeats that BIS statistics "provide little information about the geography of these payment obligations" and says the BIS is reviewing the OTCD collection to close this. Nothing has been published yet.

3. NDFs ARE INSIDE THE HEADLINE AND CANNOT BE STRIPPED OUT. An NDF settles net — a fraction of notional — so including it in a gross payment-obligation total overstates the obligation. BMM say NDFs are under 10% of turnover in these instruments and let it go. That was a defensible approximation in 2022; with EME currency trading rising sharply (USD/CNY turnover +59%, USD/HKD +95% between the 2022 and 2025 surveys) it deserves rechecking, and it cannot be rechecked in the stock data.

4. THE NON-US BANK NUMBER IS NOT REPRODUCIBLE. The $39tn is the figure that gets quoted most and it is the one you cannot rebuild. The Technical Annex names an OCC input and an LBS/CBS-derived input without identifying either line item, and the arithmetic does not close against the OCC's published FX aggregate (it would require ~$17tn of US-bank net dollar provision via FX swaps). Do not roll it forward. Publish 0.5 × global USD-leg forwards and swaps = $56.75tn as the reproducible upper component and label it as such.

5. NOTIONAL IS NOT NET OF BILATERAL NETTING. BMM footnote 7 says bilateral netting allowable under supervisory or accounting methodologies "could more than halve net interdealer payment obligations". No published series applies it. The gross figure is the right one for a settlement-risk question and the wrong one for a solvency question, and there is no netted alternative.

6. THE 2025 COVERAGE BREAK CANNOT BE QUANTIFIED. BIS tells you non-regular reporters were 13% of FX notional in June 2025 but does not tell you what the interpolation had assumed, and the absence of a reporting-jurisdiction dimension means you cannot construct a consistent-coverage series yourself. The +15.9% jump at 2025-S1 is part real, part artefact, in unknown proportions.

7. THE LBS RESIDENCE VIEW HAS NO SECTOR AND NO COUNTERPARTY COUNTRY. In the foreign-currency cut — the one that isolates banks outside the US — L_CP_SECTOR and L_CP_COUNTRY both collapse to totals. I verified this: the sector query returns a single number. So you can say the eurodollar system holds $17.86tn of cross-border USD claims but you cannot say how much is on non-banks. The sector split exists only in the by-nationality view, which in turn loses the bank-location dimension. You get nationality or location, never both.

8. SINGAPORE IS MISSING. In the USD residence cut, Singapore, Indonesia, Saudi Arabia and Malaysia return NaN under confidentiality rules. Singapore intermediates 11.8% of global FX turnover, up from 9% in 2022 — the fastest-growing major centre — and its dollar book is suppressed.

9. CHINESE BANKS ARE STRUCTURALLY UNDERSTATED. China reports its banks' cross-border positions but not their dollar positions vis-à-vis residents of China, and most other reporting countries do not report Chinese banks' dollar positions. BMM patch this by estimating local positions of banks in China as 80% of local foreign-currency deposits from national data — an assumption, not a measurement, and not reproducible from BIS data.

10. NO PRIMARY CROSS-CURRENCY BASIS SERIES EXISTS. No BIS, Fed, ECB or IMF publication carries one. BIS's own charts are sourced to Bloomberg. The price of the balance-sheet constraint — the one observable that tells you whether this channel is tight or slack — is available only from a commercial vendor. That is a striking gap for a market this size.

11. YOU CANNOT ATTRIBUTE OFFSHORE DEPOSIT CREATION TO FX SWAPS. The LBS shows offshore dollar liabilities growing 9.4% year-on-year break-adjusted. Some of that is the dollar leg of swaps being created rather than intermediated. There is no data that apportions it, and I am not going to invent an apportionment.

12. THE HEDGE RATIO IS NOT PUBLISHED. The Dec 2025 BIS analysis attributes the 2025 surge to investors entering April with low hedge ratios on dollar assets and rushing to hedge. Aggregate hedge ratios for non-US investors' dollar portfolios are not published by any official source; they are estimated from national supervisory returns (Danish, Dutch, Japanese and Korean supervisors publish partial data) or bought from vendors.

13. THE OCC SERIES IS CURRENTLY UNUSABLE AT THE MARGIN. Q1 2026 shows a 42.5% quarter-on-quarter jump in total notional and internally inconsistent bank-versus-holding-company figures for two of the largest dealers, with no break note. Until that is reconciled, any calculation using US bank derivative notional for Q1 2026 is unsafe.

---

# Margin, prime brokerage, and the valuation-as-collateral channel: credit extended against equity market value (FINRA margin debt, prime brokerage financing and internalisation, synthetic prime via single-name total return swaps, and securities-based lending at wealth managers)

**Verdict: `contested`**

## What it is

Four mechanically distinct instruments that all monetise the same underlying: the market price of equities.

(1) REGULATED MARGIN AT A BROKER-DEALER. Customer buys $200 of stock with $100 of cash in a Reg T margin account. The BD books a $100 receivable from the customer (asset) and pays $200 to the seller. Legal constraint: 12 CFR 220.12(a) sets initial margin at 50% of current market value for margin equity securities; FINRA Rule 4210(c) sets maintenance at 25%. Critically, 12 CFR 220.1(b)(3)(i) disapplies Reg T entirely "to the extent that they comply with a portfolio margining system under rules approved or amended by the SEC" — i.e. FINRA Rule 4210(g) risk-based portfolio margin, which routinely produces 6:1 or better on hedged books. So a single dollar of FINRA-reported debit balance carries wildly different implied leverage depending on account type, and the series has no split.

The BD must fund the $100 receivable. Its four sources, in the order 15c3-3 contemplates them: (a) other customers' free credit balances; (b) rehypothecation of the customer's own securities into stock loan or repo; (c) bank loans to the BD; (d) firm capital. Rule 15c3-3(a)(5) caps (b): "excess margin securities" are those whose market value exceeds 140% of the customer's debit balances, and 15c3-3(b)(3) requires those to be segregated. So a US BD may pledge customer securities up to 140% of the debit. There is no equivalent cap in most non-US prime brokerage books, which is a structural reason why offshore and synthetic books grow faster.

(2) CASH PRIME BROKERAGE. Same mechanics, institutional client, DVP/RVP or margin-account booking, plus stock-loan-financed shorts. The short leg is a funding source, not a use: the short seller's proceeds sit as a credit that finances someone else's long. This is INTERNALISATION — the dealer nets a client long against a client short and needs external funding only for the residual. No published series measures the internalised fraction anywhere in the world.

(3) SYNTHETIC PRIME / SINGLE-NAME TOTAL RETURN SWAPS. The canonical description is in the SEC's own complaint against Archegos (SEC v. Hwang, 1:22-cv-03402 SDNY, filed 27 Apr 2022): the client posts initial margin only; the dealer's Delta One desk buys the underlying shares in the market to hedge (¶36–37); the swap references a single issuer with a multi-year term and no periodic reset (¶33); the client's economic exposure never appears in a 13D/13F because it is not beneficial ownership (¶31 — Archegos ran a daily internal report specifically to keep cash equity below the 5% threshold). Balance-sheet-wise, the dealer holds the shares as inventory and the client holds a derivative. The 90% of the purchase price the client did not post has been advanced by the dealer and must be funded by the dealer — repo of the hedge shares, stock loan, a bank loan, or netting against another client's synthetic short.

(4) SECURITIES-BASED LENDING / NON-PURPOSE LOANS. A bank affiliate (Morgan Stanley Private Bank, Charles Schwab Bank, Bank of America) lends cash against a pledged securities portfolio at 50–95% LTV, on the contractual condition that proceeds are NOT used to purchase securities. That condition is what makes it non-purpose credit, exempting it from Regulation U's loan-value limits (12 CFR 221) and from Reg T. It is booked as a bank loan, not a broker-dealer receivable. It is therefore outside the FINRA collection by construction. It funds houses, tax bills, private-market capital calls and business investment — i.e. it converts equity valuation directly into spendable deposits without any securities sale and without any saving.

## Is it money creation?

SPLIT THE ANSWER BY THE FUNDING LEG. The asset side (credit against equity) is identical across all four instruments; the monetary consequence depends entirely on who supplies the cash.

CASE A — funded from other customers' free credit balances. Pure intermediation. Idle customer cash becomes the share seller's deposit. No new money. Ceiling on this channel: FINRA free credit balances were $422.4bn (cash $205.1bn + margin $217.3bn) at Jul 2026 against $1,417.2bn of debits. So at most ~30% of the FINRA margin book can be funded this way. The other ~70% is funded externally, and the ratio has deteriorated: in Dec 2022, free credits ($410bn) covered 68% of debits ($607bn).

CASE B — funded by rehypothecating the customer's own securities into stock loan or repo. The cash comes from the securities lender's cash collateral pool, which is itself typically an MMF or a cash-reinvestment vehicle. Existing money changes hands; no deposit is created. BUT the customer's securities are simultaneously (i) still shown as the customer's property and (ii) supporting a cash claim held by a third party. That is collateral-multiplier near-money, not deposit money. Morgan Stanley's own ASC 860-30-50 disclosure quantifies the reuse rate at that firm: $1,382,658mn of collateral received with the right to sell or repledge at 30 Jun 2026, of which $1,062,240mn was actually sold or repledged — 76.8%. Bank of America: $1.4tn received, $1.3tn repledged.

CASE C — funded by a commercial bank loan to the broker-dealer. THIS IS ORDINARY DEPOSIT CREATION. The bank writes a loan asset and a deposit liability; the BD pays the deposit to the share seller; the seller holds new M2. This is the case that matters most for the operator's puzzle, and it is growing fast. Z.1 Table L.130 line 23 (Security brokers and dealers; depository institution loans n.e.c.; liability, FL663168005) went $179.4bn (2023) → $352.9bn (2024:Q4) → $541.8bn (2025:Q4). It tripled in two years. The corresponding money IS inside M2 — but the credit counterpart is classified as lending to a financial intermediary, so it appears in no household, corporate or consumer credit aggregate an analyst would normally read. The operator's intuition is therefore partly right and partly wrong: the money is not invisible to M2; the CREDIT is invisible to every conventional credit aggregate. It is mis-attributed, not missing.

CASE D — synthetic, internalised. Client A wants synthetic long AAPL; client B wants synthetic short AAPL. The dealer writes both swaps and buys nothing. Zero external funding, zero new money, zero balance-sheet growth, and the dealer earns the spread on both sides. Purchasing power over the underlying has been created for A out of B's willingness to be short, with only initial margin posted by each. Nothing whatsoever is measured. This is the true blind spot and it is unmeasurable with current disclosure.

CASE E — securities-based lending at a bank. UNAMBIGUOUS DEPOSIT CREATION, and the cleanest instance of the operator's mechanism. The bank creates a loan and a deposit against a pledged portfolio the client never sells. Aggregate savings do not rise; no assets are sold; new spendable deposits appear; and because the collateral is equities, the borrowing base expands one-for-one with the valuation boom that the spending helps to sustain. Morgan Stanley's SBL book alone rose from $105.3bn to $116.4bn in six months to 30 Jun 2026. This is exactly the ZIRP-era Japanese mechanic the operator is reaching for, run against equity rather than land.

VERDICT: creates_near_money, with the honest caveat that Case C and Case E are conventional bank money creation hiding under a non-standard borrower classification, Case B is collateral-chain near-money, and Cases A and D are not creation at all. Anyone who tells you the whole channel is money creation is overselling; anyone who says it is all intermediation has not looked at L.130 line 23 or at the bank SBL books.

## Size

All figures below are AMOUNTS OUTSTANDING (STOCK) unless the line says FLOW. Vintages are stated because several of these were revised or delayed in the last year.

VISIBLE STOCK — FINRA margin debt (FINRA Rule 4521(d) collection; file downloaded and parsed 2026-08-21):
- Jul 2026: $1,417,225mn debit balances in customers' securities margin accounts. STOCK.
- Jun 2026: $1,502,072mn — the all-time high in the series, first month above $1.5tn. STOCK.
- Jul 2025: $1,022,548mn. STOCK.
- Dec 2025: $1,225,597mn; Dec 2024: $899,168mn. STOCK.
- Prior-cycle peak Oct 2021: $935,862mn; trough Dec 2022: $606,659mn. STOCK.
- Free credit balances Jul 2026: $205,132mn (cash accounts) + $217,305mn (margin accounts) = $422,437mn. STOCK.
- IMPLIED FLOW: +$394,677mn increase in the stock over the twelve months to Jul 2026. That is the annual net credit extension visible in this one series. Note the Jun→Jul 2026 fall of $84,847mn — report it as published, but FINRA restates and publishes no vintage archive, so do not treat a single month's move as final.

HEDGE FUND PRIME BROKERAGE — OFR Hedge Fund Monitor, Form PF section 2a, Qualifying Hedge Funds, reference 2026-03-31 (retrieved via the OFR API 2026-08-21):
- Borrowing from prime brokers: $3,221bn. STOCK. (2025-12-31: $3,259bn; 2024-12-31: $2,500bn.)
- Repo borrowing: $3,243bn. Other secured: $781bn. STOCK.
- Equities gross notional exposure: $8,282bn total — long $5,090bn, short $3,192bn. NOTIONAL, not credit.
- Of which equity DERIVATIVE notional: long $1,657bn + short $1,773bn = $3,430bn, i.e. 41% of equity GNE is derivative rather than cash. NOTIONAL.
- All-QHF gross notional exposure $42,235bn against GAV $12,626bn (3.35x). NOTIONAL over MARKET VALUE.
- Collateral posted by QHFs: securities $4,924bn, cash $2,921bn. STOCK.

SYNTHETIC PRIME, GLOBAL PROXY — BIS OTC derivatives statistics, semiannual, end-December 2025 (2025-S2), net-net basis, all reporting dealers, all counterparties (bulk file WS_OTC_DERIV2 downloaded 2026-08-21):
- Equity-linked FORWARDS AND SWAPS notional outstanding: $6,297bn. Against "other financial institutions" counterparties (the hedge-fund/asset-manager bucket): $4,848bn. NOTIONAL.
- End-Dec 2024: $4,674bn total / $3,576bn vs OFI. So +34.7% and +35.6% respectively. NOTIONAL.
- Total equity-linked OTC derivatives including options: $11,933bn. NOTIONAL.
- This is the closest published global proxy for synthetic prime brokerage. It is a notional, it includes index swaps and delta-one flow that is not financing, and it cannot be converted into a credit figure without an assumption about initial margin. Do not multiply it by anything and call the result credit.

NATIONAL ACCOUNTS — Federal Reserve Z.1, release of 11 June 2026, reference 2026:Q1. Prior vintages 19 Mar 2026 (2025:Q4), 9 Jan 2026 (2025:Q3 — that release slipped roughly a quarter), 11 Sep 2025 (2025:Q2). The standalone "Security credit" L-table no longer exists; security credit now sits inside Table L.216, Other loans and advances:
- L.216 line 35, Margin accounts at brokers and dealers (assets), FL663067005: $679.3bn at 2026:Q1 ($662.1bn at 2025:Q4). STOCK.
- L.216 line 36, household liability, FL663067003: $622.2bn at 2026:Q1 ($609.7bn at 2025:Q4). STOCK.
- L.216 line 38, Cash accounts at brokers and dealers (liabilities), FL663167015: $711.3bn at 2025:Q4. STOCK.
- L.130 line 23, BD borrowing from depository institutions, FL663168005: $541.8bn at 2025:Q4, from $352.9bn a year earlier and $179.4bn in 2023. STOCK. This is the deposit-creating funding leg.
- L.130 line 44, BD short positions in corporate equities, FL664164103: $242.8bn at 2025:Q4 from $148.2bn. STOCK. Proxy for delta-hedge inventory turnover.
- Z.1 hedge-fund prime brokerage margin, FL624123035: $774.0bn at 2025:Q4 from $536.5bn at 2024:Q4 (+44.3%). STOCK. Z.1's hedge-fund sector runs a quarter behind the rest of the release because it is Form PF-derived; use the OFR series instead for the current quarter.

SECURITIES-BASED LENDING, FIRM LEVEL, all at 30 June 2026 from Q2 2026 Forms 10-Q:
- Morgan Stanley (ms-20260630.htm, filed 2026-08-04): securities-based lending loans $116,431mn (investment grade $113,497mn + non-investment grade $2,934mn), from $105,278mn at 31 Dec 2025. Separately, the Wealth Management "Securities-based lending and Other" loan line is $120,444mn from $109,201mn. STOCK.
- Morgan Stanley margin loan receivables, disclosed separately as "Margin Loans and Other Lending": Institutional Securities $64,084mn + Wealth Management $36,244mn = $100,328mn, from $83,871mn at 31 Dec 2025. STOCK. Note the loan book EXCEEDS the margin book at this firm — 1.16:1.
- Bank of America (bac-20260630.htm, filed 2026-07-31): US securities-based lending loans $61.9bn, from $55.0bn at 31 Dec 2025. STOCK.
- Charles Schwab (schw-20260630.htm, filed 2026-08-07): Pledged Asset Lines $33,416mn, from $26,570mn at 31 Dec 2025 (+25.8% in six months). STOCK. Schwab's receivables from brokerage clients, which ARE inside the FINRA figure, were $122.8bn, +48% year-on-year.
- Three-firm SBL subtotal sitting entirely outside the FINRA series: $211.7bn. Adding Wells Fargo, JPMorgan, Goldman, Citi, UBS Americas, Raymond James, LPL, Northern Trust and BNY would plainly take this well past $400bn, but I have not verified those and will not estimate them.

NOT PUBLISHED, and say so plainly:
- Total US securities-based lending outstanding: no aggregate series exists.
- Total return swap notional or financing by client type or by single-name underlying: no aggregate outstanding-stock series exists.
- Internalised (netted) client exposure inside dealers: no series exists anywhere.
- Corporate borrowing base expansion attributable to equity valuation: no series exists.

**Growth.** FINRA margin debt, the one clean public series: STOCK of $1,417,225mn at Jul 2026 against $1,022,548mn at Jul 2025, i.e. +38.6% year-on-year, a twelve-month FLOW of +$394,677mn. Calendar 2025 alone: +36.3% (Dec-2025 $1,225,597mn vs Dec-2024 $899,168mn). Against the Oct-2021 cycle peak of $935,862mn the stock is +51.4%; against the Dec-2022 trough of $606,659mn it is +133.6%.

Hedge fund prime brokerage borrowing (Form PF QHF, OFR): $3,221bn at 2026-Q1 vs $2,500bn at 2024-Q4, +28.8% over five quarters. Hedge fund equity gross notional exposure: $8,282bn at 2026-Q1 vs $6,376bn at 2025-Q1, +29.9% year-on-year.

BIS equity-linked forwards and swaps notional: $6,297bn at end-Dec 2025 vs $4,674bn at end-Dec 2024, +34.7% year-on-year, net-net basis.

Broker-dealer borrowing from banks (Z.1 L.130 line 23): $541.8bn at 2025:Q4 vs $352.9bn at 2024:Q4, +53.5% year-on-year, and +202% over two years from $179.4bn.

Securities-based lending, six months to 30 Jun 2026: Morgan Stanley +10.6%, Bank of America +12.5%, Schwab Pledged Asset Lines +25.8%. Annualising a half-year is unsafe, so state the half-year.

Every one of these growth rates exceeds nominal GDP growth by a wide margin, and the fastest-growing of them (bank lending to dealers, at +53.5%) is the deposit-creating leg.

## Series

| Series | Publisher | Freq | Cost | Gives | Limitations |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Customer Margin Balances (Debit balances in customers' securities margin accounts; free credit balances in cash and margin accounts) | FINRA, collected under FINRA Rule 4521(d) via the Customer Margin Balance Form on FINRA Gateway | Monthly, as of the last business day of the month | free | Three columns, monthly from January 1997: aggregate customer debit balances in securities margin accounts; free credit balances in cash accounts; free credit balances in margin accounts. Gross, not netted. The only direct, high-frequency public measurement of equity-collateralised customer credit anywhere in the world. | Coverage is defined by Rule 4521(d) and its FAQ, and the exclusions are exactly the ones that matter. EXCLUDED: security-based swap accounts (so all synthetic prime is out by construction); DVP/RVP customer balances (a common institutional booking); omnibus accounts of other member firms; accounts of other FINRA members; associated-person accounts excluded under Rule 15c3-3. Also excluded, because they are not at a broker-dealer at all: everything booked at a bank affiliate — securities-based lending, pledged asset lines, Lombard loans. INCLUDED, which surprises people: non-purpose loans booked in good-faith accounts at the BD, and debit balances in portfolio-margin accounts, whose implied leverage is several times a Reg T account's with no flag in the data. No split by customer type, account type, collateral type or leverage. No geographic split. FINRA restates and overwrites a single file URL — there is NO vintage archive, so you must snapshot the xlsx every month yourself or you lose the ability to detect revisions. Series break at February 2010 when collection moved to Rule 4521 across all members regardless of predecessor SRO. |
| Financial Accounts of the United States (Z.1), Table L.216 Other loans and advances, lines 35–40; Table L.130 Security brokers and dealers | Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System | Quarterly | free | Sectoral allocation of margin credit. L.216 line 35 FL663067005 margin accounts at brokers and dealers (asset), line 36 FL663067003 household liability, line 37 FL663067063 broker-dealer liability, line 38 FL663167015 cash accounts at BDs (liability), line 39 FL153067005 household asset, line 40 FL263067005 rest-of-world net. L.130 gives the whole dealer balance sheet, of which line 23 FL663168005 (bank loans to dealers) is the deposit-creation funding leg, line 44 FL664164103 dealer short positions in corporate equities is the hedge-inventory proxy, and lines 3/20 give dealer repo assets and liabilities. | The standalone 'Security credit' L-table has been abolished; security credit is now folded into L.216, and legacy series such as FL763067003 (US-chartered depository institutions, security credit, asset — still live on the DDP and FRED at $536,428mn for 2026:Q1) no longer appear in any published L-table. Do not build on FL763067003 until you have confirmed its current definition in the Financial Accounts Guide. L.216 line 35 at $662.1bn for 2025:Q4 is roughly HALF the $1,225.6bn FINRA reported for Dec 2025; see build_recipe step 3 for what to do about that. Release schedule slipped in the last year: 2025:Q3 was published on 9 January 2026 rather than December. Sectoral allocations within the household line are residual estimates, not measurements. |
| Hedge funds; loans, total secured borrowing via prime brokerages (margin accounts); liability — FL624123035, plus FL623167003 domestic and FL623169533 foreign | Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System (Z.1 hedge fund sector, derived from SEC Form PF) | Quarterly | free | $774.0bn at 2025:Q4, of which $648.8bn from domestic institutions and the balance from foreign prime brokers. STOCK. | Always a quarter stale relative to the OFR series built from the same source; use OFR for the current quarter and this for the sector-consistent accounting. Covers only funds captured by Form PF, so no family offices. 'Secured borrowing via prime brokerages' is broader than margin lending against equities. |
| OFR Hedge Fund Monitor — Form PF aggregates (mnemonics FPF-BORROW_PRIMEBROKER_SUM, FPF-BORROW_REPO_SUM, FPF-BORROW_OTHERSECURED_SUM, FPF-ASSETCLASS_EQUITIES_GNE_SUM / _LGNE_SUM / _SGNE_SUM, FPF-ASSETCLASS_LEQYDERIV_SUM, FPF-ASSETCLASS_SEQYDERIV_SUM, FPF-STRATEGY_EQUITY_BORROWING_SUM, FPF-COLLATERALTYPE_SECURITIES_COLLATERAL_SUM) | Office of Financial Research, US Treasury | Quarterly | free | The single most useful dataset for this channel. Aggregated Qualifying Hedge Fund borrowings split by source (prime broker / repo / other secured), gross notional exposure by asset class split long and short, a dedicated long and short EQUITY DERIVATIVE notional pair which is the closest thing to a published synthetic-prime series, borrowing by investment strategy, collateral posted split cash vs securities, leverage ratios by fund-size decile, and counterparty concentration. Also carries the Fed's Senior Credit Officer Opinion Survey net-diffusion series (SCOOS-NET_HF_LEVERAGEUSE, SCOOS-NET_HF_CREDITTERMS), which run through 2026-Q2 and tell you whether dealers are tightening. | Qualifying Hedge Funds only — advisers with at least $1.5bn in hedge fund AUM, reporting quarterly on Form PF section 2a. Family offices file nothing, which is precisely the Archegos hole. Gross notional exposure is a notional, not credit, and Form PF's GNE definition sums absolute notionals across asset classes with no netting — it is a risk-scale indicator, not a balance-sheet quantity. 'Borrowing from prime brokers' does not separate borrowing against equities from borrowing against bonds. Form PF was amended in 2024–25 and definitional breaks may exist in the back series; check the OFR metadata notes before charting a long history. |
| SEC Private Funds Statistics (Form PF aggregate tables) | SEC Division of Investment Management / DERA | Quarterly | free | The SEC's own tabulation of the same Form PF filings the OFR aggregates, with additional cuts including borrowings by creditor type and the qualifying-hedge-fund population count. Useful as an independent check on the OFR numbers rather than as a substitute. | Slower than the OFR monitor and less granular on equity derivatives. Same Form PF population limits. |
| BIS OTC derivatives statistics, equity-linked contracts: forwards and swaps, notional amounts outstanding (dataflow WS_OTC_DERIV2) | Bank for International Settlements | Semiannual (end-June and end-December) | free | The only global aggregate of equity derivative notional. Filter DER_RISK='E: Equity', DER_TYPE='A: Outstanding - notional amounts', DER_INSTR='C: Forwards and swaps', DER_REP_CTY='5J: All countries', DER_BASIS='C: Net - net', DER_ISSUE_MAT='A: Total (all maturities)', DER_SECTOR_UDL='A: Total'. Vary DER_SECTOR_CPY between 'A: Total (all counterparties)' and 'C: Other financial institutions' to isolate the buy-side book. | NOTIONAL, not credit and not exposure. Mixes single-name TRS used for synthetic prime with index swaps, delta-one ETF flow and corporate hedges. Semiannual and five months stale, so useless for turning points. Reporting population is roughly seventy dealers across a dozen jurisdictions — a non-reporting dealer's book is simply absent. The net-net basis removes inter-dealer double counting between reporters but not between a reporter and a non-reporter. There is no split by underlying single name, by client type, or by initial margin, so the notional cannot be converted into a financing figure without an assumption you would be making up. |
| Form X-17A-5 annual audited reports (public portion: Statement of Financial Condition) for individual broker-dealers | SEC, filed on EDGAR | Annual, with the public portion filed within 60 days of fiscal year end | free | Firm-by-firm 'Receivables from customers' and 'Payables to customers' on the audited Statement of Financial Condition, i.e. the margin book of every US registered broker-dealer including the prime brokerage subsidiaries of the foreign banks. This is the only public route to a firm-level decomposition of the FINRA aggregate, and the only route to seeing what share sits at Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC, Morgan Stanley & Co. LLC, J.P. Morgan Securities LLC, BofA Securities, Citigroup Global Markets, UBS Securities and Barclays Capital. | Annual only, and mostly filed as unstructured PDF despite the January 2025 electronic-filing rule (90 FR 7250, effective 24 March 2025), so building a panel means OCR or hand entry. Only the Statement of Financial Condition is public: FOCUS Part II monthly line items, and the entire Rule 15c3-3a Exhibit A reserve computation, are non-public under Rule 17a-5(e)(3). Fiscal year ends differ across firms. |
| Securities-based lending disclosures in Forms 10-Q and 10-K of the large wealth managers | SEC EDGAR, filed by the issuers | Quarterly | free | The only actual dollar measurements of securities-based lending that exist. Morgan Stanley discloses SBL split investment grade and non-investment grade in the credit-quality-by-vintage table, AND separately discloses margin loan receivables split Institutional Securities versus Wealth Management, so you can see the bank-book and broker-book legs side by side at one firm. Bank of America discloses US securities-based lending loans in the Direct/Indirect Consumer footnote. Schwab discloses Pledged Asset Lines as a distinct bank loan class alongside receivables from brokerage clients. All three also carry the ASC 860-30-50 collateral received / repledged pair. | Definitions are not harmonised. Morgan Stanley's 'Securities-based lending and Other' mixes pure SBL with bespoke ultra-high-net-worth facilities including direct commercial real estate lending, which the firm quantifies separately ($7.1bn at 30 Jun 2026). Bank of America buries SBL in a footnote to a portfolio labelled Direct/Indirect Consumer. Schwab's PAL line is clean but Schwab is atypical. No firm discloses collateral composition, LTV distribution, or how much of the loan proceeds left the securities complex. There is no way to build a national total from these, only a floor. |
| FFIEC Consolidated Reports of Condition and Income Schedule RC-C Part I item 9.b.(1) 'Loans for purchasing or carrying securities (secured and unsecured)', and the FR Y-9C equivalent Schedule HC-C | FFIEC / Federal Reserve | Quarterly | free | The bank-side purpose-credit line. Every insured depository reports it, so it is a genuine census rather than a sample. | This line captures only Regulation U PURPOSE credit — loans whose proceeds go to buying or carrying margin stock. Almost all wealth-manager SBL is deliberately structured as NON-PURPOSE precisely to escape Reg U's loan-value limits, and is therefore booked in item 9.b.(2) 'All other loans' or in consumer loan categories, where it is indistinguishable from anything else. So this series systematically misses the fastest-growing part of the channel. I was unable to retrieve the current form to verify the MDRM mnemonic (ffiec.gov returns 403 to programmatic access); confirm the code — historically RCON/RCFD 1545 and BHCK 1545 — against the current Micro Data Reference Manual before you build on it. |
| Senior Credit Officer Opinion Survey on Dealer Financing Terms (SCOOS) | Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System | Quarterly | free | Dealers' own reports on whether hedge fund clients' use of leverage rose or fell, whether credit terms tightened, and how hard clients pushed back in negotiation. It is the only forward-looking read on the unmeasured part of the channel. Net readings at 2026-Q2: leverage use 0.0, credit terms +5.0, collateral terms 0.0 — i.e. dealers report neither tightening nor loosening even as the underlying stocks grow 30–50% a year. | Diffusion indices, not quantities. Around twenty respondents. Reports direction of change, never level. A zero reading is consistent with terms staying loose. |
| FINRA Securities Lending and Transparency Engine (SLATE), FINRA Rule 6500 series under SEC Rule 10c-1a | FINRA | Will be transaction-level reporting with daily aggregate dissemination | free | When live, loan-by-loan reporting of securities loans including rates, terms and collateral, with daily aggregates disseminated. It will illuminate the stock-loan leg that funds shorts and rehypothecation, but not the swap leg. | Not available. Do not plan any 2026 analysis around it. Even once live, borrower identity will not be disseminated, and equity finance conducted as a swap rather than a loan is out of scope entirely. |

## Build recipe

STEP 1 — BUILD THE VISIBLE SERIES AND KNOW EXACTLY WHAT IT IS.
Pull https://www.finra.org/sites/default/files/2021-03/margin-statistics.xlsx . It is a single-sheet xlsx with inline strings and no sharedStrings part, so openpyxl or a five-line ElementTree parse over xl/worksheets/sheet1.xml both work. Columns: Year-Month, Debit Balances in Customers' Securities Margin Accounts, Free Credit Balances in Customers' Cash Accounts, Free Credit Balances in Customers' Securities Margin Accounts. Units are USD millions. 356 rows back to 1997-01. IMMEDIATELY copy the file to a dated archive path — FINRA overwrites this single URL every month and publishes no vintage history, so revision detection is your responsibility alone. Diff each new download against the previous one and log any restated month.
Construct three derived series: (a) the STOCK of debits; (b) the twelve-month FLOW, being the first difference over twelve months, which is the actual net credit extension — +$394,677mn to July 2026; (c) net customer credit, debits minus the two free-credit columns, $994,788mn at July 2026, which is the amount of the margin book that other customers' idle cash cannot fund. Series (c) collapsing toward zero would mean the book is self-funded; it is instead widening, which is your first evidence that external funding is doing the work.
Do NOT deflate any of this by market capitalisation to produce a "margin debt as a share of market cap" ratio. Market cap is a price times a share count and moves for reasons that have nothing to do with credit; the ratio will fall in a melt-up and tell you nothing.

STEP 2 — ESTABLISH THE COVERAGE PERIMETER BEFORE YOU COMPARE ANYTHING TO IT.
Read the Rule 4521(d) FAQ at https://www.finra.org/rules-guidance/key-topics/margin-accounts/frequently-asked-questions-under-finra-rule-4521d and write down its exclusion list, because it defines the shape of the hole: security-based swap accounts, DVP/RVP customer balances, omnibus accounts of other member firms, accounts of other FINRA members, associated-person accounts excluded under 15c3-3. Note the two counter-intuitive INCLUSIONS: non-purpose loans booked in good-faith accounts at the broker-dealer are reportable, and portfolio-margin debits are in there with no flag. Then read 12 CFR 220.1(b)(3)(i) and 12 CFR 220.12(a) from the eCFR versioner API (https://www.ecfr.gov/api/versioner/v1/full/{date}/title-12.xml?part=220) to confirm that Reg T's 50% initial margin does not apply at all to portfolio-margined relationships. The conclusion you must carry forward: one dollar of FINRA debit balance in a Reg T account and one dollar in a portfolio-margin account represent very different quantities of leverage, and the series cannot distinguish them.

STEP 3 — RECONCILE FINRA TO Z.1 AND DO NOT ADD THEM.
Pull Z.1 Table L.216 from https://www.federalreserve.gov/releases/z1/{release_date}/html/l216.htm (the accessible/ path is dead; use html/). Release dates are enumerated at /releases/z1/release-dates.htm — currently 20260611 (2026:Q1), 20260319 (2025:Q4), 20260109 (2025:Q3), 20250911 (2025:Q2). Take line 35 FL663067005 and note it reads $662.1bn for 2025:Q4 against FINRA's $1,225.6bn for Dec 2025 — a $563.5bn gap on the same reference date.
The gap is a classification difference, not an error, and the resolution is the point of the exercise. Z.1 confines "margin accounts at brokers and dealers" to two counterpart sectors, households (line 36, $609.7bn) and other broker-dealers (line 37, $52.4bn), which sum exactly to line 35. Hedge fund borrowing from prime brokers is NOT in there: it is carried in the hedge-fund sector as FL624123035, "secured borrowing via prime brokerages", at $774.0bn for the same quarter. FINRA's collection, by contrast, sweeps every customer type together. So FINRA ≈ Z.1 household+dealer margin PLUS the portion of hedge-fund PB margin booked in margin accounts at US broker-dealers, MINUS DVP/RVP and omnibus balances. Note that $662.1bn + $774.0bn = $1,436.1bn overshoots FINRA's $1,225.6bn, which tells you the hedge-fund series also contains foreign prime brokers ($125.2bn, being FL624123035 less FL623167003) and non-margin-account secured borrowing. Write the reconciliation down as an inequality with named residuals; do not force it to close, and never publish the sum.

STEP 4 — MEASURE THE FUNDING LEG, WHICH IS WHERE MONEY IS OR IS NOT CREATED.
From Z.1 Table L.130 take line 23 FL663168005, broker-dealer borrowing from depository institutions: $179.4bn (2023) → $352.9bn (2024:Q4) → $541.8bn (2025:Q4). This is the deposit-creating leg and it is the fastest-growing number in this whole channel. Also take line 20 FL662151003 dealer repo liabilities ($2,827.8bn) and line 3 FL662051003 repo assets ($1,989.6bn) — the net repo book funds inventory including delta-one hedges, but repo overlaps another researcher's channel so use it as context only. Take line 44 FL664164103, dealer short positions in corporate equities, $242.8bn from $148.2bn, as the delta-hedge inventory proxy.
Then compute the funding decomposition of the margin book directly: FINRA debits minus FINRA free credits gives the externally-funded residual; compare its growth to the growth of L.130 line 23. If bank lending to dealers is growing faster than the externally-funded residual, the money-creating share of the channel is rising. On the numbers above it is.

STEP 5 — SIZE THE HEDGE FUND / PRIME BROKERAGE LEG FROM FORM PF.
Hit the OFR API with no key. Enumerate series at https://data.financialresearch.gov/hf/v1/metadata/mnemonics/ (497 mnemonics), then pull each of FPF-BORROW_PRIMEBROKER_SUM, FPF-BORROW_REPO_SUM, FPF-BORROW_OTHERSECURED_SUM, FPF-ASSETCLASS_EQUITIES_LGNE_SUM, FPF-ASSETCLASS_EQUITIES_SGNE_SUM, FPF-ASSETCLASS_LEQYDERIV_SUM, FPF-ASSETCLASS_SEQYDERIV_SUM, FPF-STRATEGY_EQUITY_BORROWING_SUM and FPF-COLLATERALTYPE_SECURITIES_COLLATERAL_SUM from https://data.financialresearch.gov/hf/v1/series/timeseries/?mnemonic={M} . Each returns a JSON array of [date, value] with values in whole dollars.
The single most important derived quantity in this whole exercise is the SYNTHETIC SHARE OF HEDGE FUND EQUITY EXPOSURE: (LEQYDERIV + SEQYDERIV) / EQUITIES_GNE. At 2026-Q1 that is ($1,657bn + $1,773bn) / $8,282bn = 41.4%. At 2024-Q4 it was ($1,149bn + $1,137bn) / $6,179bn = 37.0%. So the derivative share is rising, and by 2026-Q1 roughly two fifths of hedge fund equity exposure is held in a form that generates no FINRA margin debt and no 13F holding. This ratio is your headline finding and it is computed entirely from published primary data.
Also compute long equity GNE ($5,090bn at 2026-Q1) against FINRA total margin debt ($1,304bn at Apr 2026, $1,416bn at May): hedge funds alone hold roughly four times the long equity exposure that the entire FINRA margin book could finance. That is a comparison of NOTIONAL to CREDIT and must be labelled as such every time it is stated.

STEP 6 — CALIBRATE THE VISIBLE FRACTION USING ARCHEGOS, WHICH IS THE ONLY CASE WHERE THE FULL POSITION WAS EVENTUALLY DISCLOSED.
Download the SEC's complaint from https://www.sec.gov/files/litigation/complaints/2022/comp-pr2022-70.pdf (sec.gov requires a User-Agent carrying a contact e-mail; a bare curl gets 403) and pdftotext it. The load-bearing paragraphs are: ¶50, at 31 March 2020 approximately $1.6bn invested capital on approximately $10.2bn gross exposures; ¶51, at 1 January 2021 approximately $7.7bn on approximately $54bn; ¶52, at 22 March 2021 over $36bn on over $160bn. ¶28 puts leverage at 400–700% and sometimes 1000% across roughly 100 issuers. ¶31 records that Archegos ran a daily internal report tracking cash equity holdings against each issuer's shares outstanding for the express purpose of never crossing the 5% beneficial-ownership disclosure threshold. ¶33 says the exposure was predominantly single-name total return security-based swaps with two-year terms and no periodic reset. ¶36 says counterparties executed through Delta One desks. ¶37 says counterparties bought the referenced shares in the market to hedge. ¶32, ¶38 and ¶39 record roughly a dozen counterparties, each of which could see its own book and had "limited, and in some cases no, visibility" into the rest.
The calibration you extract: at the peak, of $160bn of gross exposure, the fraction visible in FINRA margin debt was approximately zero, because swap exposure is excluded from Rule 4521(d) by construction; the fraction visible in 13F/13D was capped at just under 5% of each issuer by deliberate design; and the fraction visible to any single counterparty was one-twelfth of the whole. Do not generalise the 0% visible fraction to the market — Archegos was an extreme, and the SEC also alleged manipulation. Use it instead as an existence proof of the upper bound of invisibility, and use the Form PF derivative share from Step 5 (41.4%) as the estimate of the typical case. Note explicitly that Archegos was a FAMILY OFFICE and therefore filed no Form PF at all under Advisers Act Rule 202(a)(11)(G)-1, so even the Form PF figure would have missed it entirely. That hole is still open.

STEP 7 — BUILD THE SECURITIES-BASED LENDING FLOOR FIRM BY FIRM, BECAUSE NO AGGREGATE EXISTS.
For each firm, get the filing index from https://data.sec.gov/submissions/CIK{10-digit-zero-padded}.json , find the latest 10-Q accession and primary document, fetch https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/{cik}/{accession-no-dashes}/{doc} with a descriptive User-Agent, strip tags and regex the relevant tables.
Morgan Stanley (CIK 0000895421, ms-20260630.htm, filed 2026-08-04): take the credit-quality-by-vintage table which splits "Securities-based lending" investment grade and non-investment grade from "Other" — $113,497mn + $2,934mn = $116,431mn of pure SBL at 30 Jun 2026, against $102,441mn + $2,837mn = $105,278mn at 31 Dec 2025. Separately take the "Margin Loans and Other Lending" table: Institutional Securities $64,084mn + Wealth Management $36,244mn = $100,328mn. These two are DISJOINT — one is a bank/other loan, the other a broker-dealer receivable — and the loan book is the larger of the two. That single firm-level fact is the cleanest available demonstration that the bank leg of this channel now exceeds the broker leg.
Bank of America (CIK 0000070858, bac-20260630.htm, filed 2026-07-31): the number is in a footnote to the loans table — "U.S. securities-based lending loans of $61.9 billion and $55.0 billion" at 30 Jun 2026 and 31 Dec 2025.
Charles Schwab (CIK 0000316709, schw-20260630.htm, filed 2026-08-07): the bank loans credit-quality table gives Pledged Asset Lines of $33,416mn at 30 Jun 2026 against $26,570mn at 31 Dec 2025, and the MD&A gives receivables from brokerage clients of $122.8bn, +48% year-on-year.
Extend the panel to Wells Fargo, JPMorgan, Goldman Sachs, Citigroup, UBS Americas, Raymond James, LPL Financial, Northern Trust and BNY. Present the result as a NAMED-FIRM FLOOR with the constituent list attached, never as an estimate of the national total. The three firms above alone give $211.7bn sitting entirely outside the FINRA series.
While you are in each 10-Q, capture the ASC 860-30-50 pair — collateral received with the right to sell or repledge, and collateral actually sold or repledged. Morgan Stanley: $1,382,658mn and $1,062,240mn, a 76.8% reuse rate. Bank of America: $1.4tn and $1.3tn. This is the collateral-multiplier leg and it is disclosed on a consistent basis across all US dealers.

STEP 8 — BOUND THE SYNTHETIC LEG GLOBALLY, WITH THE RIGHT CAVEATS.
Download https://data.bis.org/static/bulk/WS_OTC_DERIV2_csv_flat.zip . Filter DER_RISK starting 'E', DER_TYPE starting 'A' (outstanding notional), DER_INSTR starting 'C' (forwards and swaps), DER_REP_CTY '5J', DER_CPC '5J', DER_ISSUE_MAT 'A', DER_SECTOR_UDL 'A', DER_BASIS 'C' (net-net). Values are USD millions. You get $6,297,469mn at 2025-S2 against $4,674,345mn at 2024-S2. Switch DER_SECTOR_CPY from 'A' to 'C: Other financial institutions' for the buy-side cut: $4,847,528mn against $3,575,532mn.
State the caveat in the same sentence as the number every time: this is NOTIONAL on the dealer's books, it mixes single-name synthetic prime with index swaps and corporate hedges, it is semiannual and five months stale, and converting it to credit would require an initial-margin assumption that no published source supports. Its legitimate use is as a growth rate and as an order-of-magnitude bound, not as an addend.

STEP 9 — THE CORPORATE BORROWING-BASE LEG: SAY THAT NO SERIES EXISTS, THEN BUILD A PROXY FROM FILINGS.
There is no published series for corporate credit capacity created by equity valuation. Three constructible proxies, all from EDGAR full-text search at https://efts.sec.gov/LATEST/search-index with query parameters q, forms, startdt, enddt (JSON, returns hits.total.value and per-document metadata):
(a) Insider and affiliate share pledging. Regulation S-K Item 403(b) requires the beneficial-ownership table footnotes in DEF 14A to identify shares pledged as security. Query q="pledged as collateral", forms="DEF 14A" — 228 hits for 2026 to 21 August, 241 for full-year 2024. The disclosure is qualitative (share counts, sometimes not even that; no loan amounts), so you can build a COUNT and a PLEDGED-SHARE-COUNT panel but not a dollar loan total. Say so.
(b) Variable prepaid forwards and collared monetisations, which convert a concentrated stake into cash without a taxable sale. Query q="variable prepaid forward" across all forms — 95 hits for 2026 YTD. Read the 8-K and SC 13D/A Item 6 disclosures individually; there is no aggregation.
(c) Corporate use of total return swaps. Query q="total return swap", forms="10-Q" — 136 hits for 2026 YTD. Mostly financial issuers; read them.
Be explicit in the write-up that (a), (b) and (c) are counts of disclosures, not measurements of credit, and that the true aggregate is unknown and unknowable from public data.

STEP 10 — RECORD THE REGULATORY PLUMBING THAT DETERMINES WHAT WILL AND WILL NOT BE MEASURABLE.
From the eCFR versioner API, https://www.ecfr.gov/api/versioner/v1/full/{date}/title-17.xml?part=240&section=240.15c3-3 and &section=240.15c3-3a, pull and quote: 15c3-3(a)(5), which defines excess margin securities as those whose market value exceeds 140% of the customer's debit balances, and 15c3-3(b)(3), which requires them to be segregated — this is the rehypothecation cap and it is the reason a US broker-dealer's reuse of customer collateral is bounded while its offshore affiliate's is not. And 15c3-3(e)(3)(i)(B)(1), which requires a broker-dealer with average total credits of $500 million or more to compute the customer and PAB reserve DAILY rather than weekly, with "average total credits" defined as the arithmetic mean of Total Credits in the two computations reported in the twelve most recently filed month-end Forms X-17A-5.
Exhibit A (17 CFR 240.15c3-3a) gives you the exact line items: credit item 1 is free credit balances and other credit balances in customers' security accounts; debit item 10 is debit balances in customers' cash and margin accounts excluding unsecured and doubtful accounts. These are the definitional twins of the two FINRA columns, and they are the reason the FINRA collection can be trusted as a measurement — firms are already computing these numbers for the reserve formula.
Dates and citations for the write-up: the daily-computation amendments were adopted at 90 FR 2790 (13 January 2025), effective 14 March 2025, and also cut the aggregate debit reduction from 3% to 2% for daily filers; the compliance date was then extended from 31 December 2025 to 30 JUNE 2026 by 90 FR 27990 (1 July 2025). Electronic filing of FOCUS reports on EDGAR was mandated at 90 FR 7250 (21 January 2025), effective 24 March 2025. FINRA's SLATE Rule 6500 series was approved at 90 FR 1563 (8 January 2025) and pushed back by SEC temporary exemptive relief at 90 FR 36087 (31 July 2025); reporting is now understood to begin 28 September 2026 and dissemination 29 March 2027 — confirm both against the operative text of the order before relying on them.

STEP 11 — ASSEMBLE THE OUTPUT AS A LADDER, NEVER A SUM.
Present five rungs, each labelled with its measurement basis, and refuse to total them:
