Between benchmarks the sector is a smoothed extrapolation. Sector total loans have been flat to falling since 2024. |
| Z.1 table S125s1.3.s — Issuers of asset-backed securities, stocks outstanding (formerly L.127) | Federal Reserve Board | Quarterly | free | Assets and liabilities of US ABS issuers. FL673065105/405/505 (1-4 family, multifamily, commercial mortgages), FL673069005 (other loans and advances, incl. domestically-issued CLO collateral), FL673070003 (securitised trade receivables), FL673090543 (funding agreements with life insurers), FL673065123 (securitised home equity). | Excludes US CLO collateral where the CLO was issued offshore — that sits in table S2.s line 15 (rest of the world). Consumer credit line is implausibly small (14.9) because most consumer ABS is consolidated back onto the sponsor under ASC 810. |
| Z.1 tables F4.5.s / F4.5a.s / F4.5b.s / F4.5c.s — Mortgages by holder (formerly L.217/218/219/220) | Federal Reserve Board | Quarterly | free | Every mortgage dollar allocated to a holding sector. FL413065015 isolates Ginnie Mae pools — the non-bank-issued segment. FL643065173/573 give mREIT residential/commercial holdings. | THERE IS NO LINE FOR CRE DEBT FUNDS OR PRIVATE DEBT FUNDS. Their holdings are absorbed into the household residual. Also: allocation is by holder of the loan, not by originator, so the non-bank ORIGINATION share is invisible here — use HMDA for that. |
| Z.1 tables F81.s and F81.t — Trade credit, stocks and transactions (formerly L.230 / F.230) | Federal Reserve Board | Quarterly | free | FL103170005 / FA103170005 — nonfinancial corporate trade payables, stock and SAAR flow. The single largest and least-watched credit aggregate in this brief. | A -889.3bn discrepancy between total payables and total receivables at 2026:Q1 (~9% of stock). Both sides are derived largely from the Census QFR and IRS SOI, and SOI runs three years behind (through 2023). Quarterly flows are extremely volatile; never quote one quarter's SAAR as a trend. |
| Z.1 old-to-new table number crosswalk | Federal Reserve Board | One-off, with the June 2026 release | free | 205-row CSV mapping every legacy Z.1 table number to the new SNA alphanumeric code. Essential: any recipe, script or note written before 11 June 2026 that references L.128, L.127, L.222 etc. will no longer resolve. | Maps table numbers only. Also read https://www.federalreserve.gov/releases/z1/z1_technical_qa.htm for the fed-funds-and-repo reclassification into total loans and the direct-investment intercompany debt move. |
| G.19 Consumer Credit | Federal Reserve Board | Monthly | free | Consumer credit outstanding by major holder and by type, monthly. The finance-company line is the highest-frequency non-bank consumer credit series available. | EXCLUDES all real-estate-secured debt and EXCLUDES BNPL. Finance company line benchmarked to the quinquennial FR 3033s and indicated monthly by FR 2248. The nonfinancial business holder line was discontinued in 2025. Revisions at each benchmark are large. |
| SEC XBRL frames / companyfacts / Financial Statement Data Sets — us-gaap:SupplierFinanceProgramObligation and rollforward tags | US Securities and Exchange Commission | Quarterly, as filers report (10-Q/10-K) | free | Firm-level outstanding reverse-factoring obligations, mandated by FASB ASU 2022-04 (ASC 405-50). Companion flow tags SupplierFinanceProgramObligationIncrease and SupplierFinanceProgramObligationDecreaseSettlement. This is the only bottom-up, name-by-name measurement of reverse factoring that exists anywhere. | US SEC registrants only — private companies, and foreign issuers filing 20-F under IFRS, are outside it. The frames API only matches EXACT calendar period ends, so non-calendar fiscal years are silently dropped: the CY2025Q3I frame returns 11 filers versus 109 in CY2026Q1I, which is a coverage artefact and not economics. Co-registrant parents and subsidiaries double-count (NextEra, Charter/CCO Holdings). Only ~26 of 122 filers tag the rollforward. Disclosure is of the buyer's obligation, not the funder's exposure. |
| SEC XBRL — us-gaap:ServicingAssetAtFairValueAmount and us-gaap:ServicingAsset | US Securities and Exchange Commission | Quarterly | free | Firm-level MSR carrying values for every SEC registrant. Pair with SIC 6162 (mortgage bankers and loan correspondents) to isolate non-banks from banks. | Two mutually exclusive tags (fair value vs amortisation method) that must be unioned, and a few filers use both for different cohorts. MSR carrying value is an INTANGIBLE VALUATION, not a credit stock — never add it to loan balances. Highly rate-sensitive: MSR marks rise when rates rise, which mechanically inflates borrowing capacity precisely when origination volume falls. |
| SEC XBRL — us-gaap:NotesReceivableNet and us-gaap:FinancingReceivableExcludingAccruedInterestBeforeAllowanceForCreditLoss | US Securities and Exchange Commission | Quarterly | free | Captive and vendor finance receivables at firm level. Captive finance subsidiaries (Ford Motor Credit, Toyota Motor Credit, GM Financial, Caterpillar Financial, John Deere Capital, American Honda Finance, PACCAR Financial) file their OWN 10-Ks because they issue public debt, so the captive balance sheet is visible separately from the parent. | The tag is not a captive filter — Capital One, Goldman Sachs, Synchrony, MetLife and Prudential dominate it. You must intersect with a hand-built captive CIK list or with SIC 6141/6153/6159. Several captives use the FinancingReceivable* tags instead, so union both. Technology vendors (Dell, HPE, Cisco) report only the on-balance-sheet portion; vendor-ARRANGED third-party financing is disclosed in narrative only, if at all. |
| FSOC Report on Nonbank Mortgage Servicing | Financial Stability Oversight Council / US Treasury | One-off (2024) | free | The only public quantification of warehouse and MSR line capacity, committed/uncommitted split, advance rates, and NMC leverage distribution. Also the authoritative non-bank origination and servicing shares. | One-off, not a series, and already 2.5 years stale. The underlying figures come from the MBFRF, which is NOT public. Market shares are from Inside Mortgage Finance (paid) and cover only the 50 largest servicers. Warehouse figures are LIMITS, not drawn balances. |
| Mortgage Bankers Financial Reporting Form (MBFRF) | Ginnie Mae / Fannie Mae / Freddie Mac (joint form) | Quarterly | confidential/regulatory | Full quarterly financials, warehouse facility inventory with committed/uncommitted flags, and MSR line detail for 550+ non-bank mortgage companies. This is the single best dataset on the channel. | NOT PUBLIC in any form. Only tabulations released by Ginnie Mae inside FSOC or FHFA publications ever reach the outside world. There is no FOIA route to firm-level data and no aggregate release schedule. |
| NMLS Mortgage Call Report (MCR) | Conference of State Bank Supervisors / NMLS | Quarterly | free | State-licensed mortgage company origination volume, servicing portfolio and, in the Financial Condition component, lines of credit including warehouse capacity. This is the public counterpart to the MBFRF and is the source FSOC used for the 9.5-creditors-per-NMC figure. | Only state-licensed entities — depository subsidiaries are out. Aggregate publications are less granular than the underlying filings. Site was not scriptable from this environment; retrieve manually. |
| Home Mortgage Disclosure Act loan/application register (LAR) | CFPB / FFIEC | Annual | free | Every covered origination with the originating institution's LEI and its type. This is THE primary source for the non-bank origination share; the Urban Institute and MBA chartbooks are derivatives of it. | Annual and slow. Covers ~90% of residential originations. Reporting thresholds were raised, cutting small-lender coverage. Business-purpose loans (i.e. residential transition / fix-and-flip) are largely EXCLUDED — so HMDA cannot see the RTL channel at all. The data-browser API is Akamai-protected and refused scripted requests from this environment; use a browser session. |
| FSB Global Monitoring Report on Nonbank Financial Intermediation 2025 | Financial Stability Board | Annual, each December | free | The only consistent cross-jurisdiction measure of this channel. EF2 ($6.1trn) is exactly finance companies plus leasing and factoring; EF5 ($5.3trn) is securitisation vehicles. New for 2025: EF2 exposures split by product type, and finance-company CRE exposure (Box 2, $4.4trn total non-bank real estate exposure). | Twelve-month data lag. Jurisdictions are anonymised in most graphs. Numbers rest on national submissions with differing methodologies — Box 2 acknowledges totals exceeding 100% of finance-company assets in some jurisdictions. Private credit funds are EXCLUDED from the narrow measure by design, which the FSB states explicitly. Text/table inconsistency on EF5 growth (4.3% vs 6.6%). |
| CFPB report on consumer use of Buy Now, Pay Later | Consumer Financial Protection Bureau | One-off | free | The only authoritative US BNPL volumes: 277.3m originated loans and $33.8bn gross merchandise value in 2022 across Affirm, Afterpay, Klarna, PayPal, Sezzle and Zip (Table 1), from a 12 U.S.C. 5512(c)(4) market-monitoring order. | DATA YEAR IS 2022 — four years stale. GMV is an annual purchase FLOW, not an outstanding stock, and no stock is published. Six firms only; excludes bank-issued instalment products, Apple Pay Later, and pay-in-30 offerings. The CFPB has not repeated the collection and the 2024 BNPL interpretive rule is no longer being enforced, so no successor series is in prospect. |
| BIS CGFS Papers No 50, Trade finance: developments and issues | Bank for International Settlements, Committee on the Global Financial System | One-off | free | The BIS's only sizing of global trade finance: US$6.5–8trn bank-intermediated during 2011, ~US$2.8trn of letters of credit (SWIFT MT700), ICC Trade Register US$1,958bn from 21 banks, Berne Union trade credit insurance ~US$1.7trn. | TWELVE YEARS OLD AND NEVER REFRESHED. It predates the entire reverse-factoring and fintech supply-chain-finance build-out and the Greensill cycle. Do not present these as current. BIS locational and consolidated banking statistics do NOT isolate trade finance, so there is no BIS series to update it with. |
| Burford Capital Limited SEC filings (CIK 0001714174) | US Securities and Exchange Commission / Burford Capital Ltd | Quarterly | free | The only continuous public balance sheet in litigation finance. Total assets $6,641m (2025-12-31 10-K), $4,270m (2026-03-31 10-Q), $4,272m (2026-06-30 10-Q); assets at fair value $5,864m / $3,353m / $3,357m. | ONE FIRM. It is not the industry and its share is unknown because no industry aggregate exists. Dual-tags under both ifrs-full and us-gaap namespaces with the older 20-F facts under IFRS, so a naive companyfacts pull mixes bases. The large Q1 2026 contraction requires reading the 10-Q before it is characterised. |

## Build recipe

STEP 0 — FIX THE Z.1 TABLE NUMBERS FIRST. Effective the 11 June 2026 release, every Z.1 table was renumbered to SNA alphanumeric codes. Any existing script referencing L.128 will silently fail. Download https://www.federalreserve.gov/releases/z1/current/z1_table_mapping.csv (205 rows, columns new,old,title) and remap. The mappings for this channel are: L.128→S125s2.1.s and F.128→S125s2.1.t (finance companies); L.127→S125s1.3.s (ABS issuers); L.126→S125s1.2.s (agency/GSE mortgage pools); L.129→S124.4.s (mREITs); L.131→S127.1.s (holding companies); L.132→S127.2.s (other financial business); L.216→F4.6.s (other loans and advances); L.218→F4.5a.s (1-4 family); L.219→F4.5b.s (multifamily); L.220→F4.5c.s (commercial mortgages); L.222→F4.3.s (consumer credit); L.230→F81.s (trade credit). CRITICAL AND USEFUL: the underlying FL/FA/LM series identifiers are UNCHANGED. Build against series IDs, not table numbers, and the renumbering costs you nothing ever again. Also note in the same release: federal funds and repo were reclassified from a top-level instrument into a subcategory of total loans (F4.1.t/.s under F4.t/.s); "levels" was renamed "stocks outstanding"; "monetary authority" became "central bank"; and the Financial Accounts Guide Tables webpage was retired.

STEP 1 — PULL THE Z.1 BACKBONE. Download https://www.federalreserve.gov/releases/z1/current/z1_csv_files.zip (one CSV per table, plus a data dictionary). Extract these series and label each STOCK (FL/LM prefix) or FLOW (FA prefix, seasonally adjusted annual rate):
  Non-bank mortgage holdings: FL613065105 (finance co 1-4 family), FL673065105 (ABS issuer 1-4 family), FL643065173 (mREIT 1-4 family), FL413065015 (Ginnie Mae pools), FL673065505 (ABS issuer commercial), FL643065573 (mREIT commercial), FL673065123 (ABS issuer home equity).
  Non-bank consumer credit: FL613066005 (finance co), FL673066000 (ABS issuer), FL613066303 (consumer operating leases, MEMO — not counted as household debt anywhere).
  Non-bank business lending: FL613069505 (finance co loans to business), FL673069803 (ABS issuer loans to NFC, i.e. domestic CLO collateral), FL153069803 (household-residual holdings of loans to NFC — the Z.1's private-credit blind spot).
  Warehouse proxy: FL613168005 (depository institution loans n.e.c. owed by finance companies). This is your drawn-warehouse series. $289.8bn at 2026:Q1 versus $235.3bn at end-2023.
  Trade credit: FL103170005 and FA103170005 (NFC payables stock and flow), FL103070005/FA103070005 (receivables), FL673070003 (securitised receivables at ABS issuers), FL903070005 (the discrepancy — carry it visibly, do not suppress it).
Build the quarterly panel from 2000 forward. Re-pull every quarter: the Z.1 revises the full history, and the finance-company and trade-credit blocks revise materially.

STEP 2 — BUILD THE REVERSE-FACTORING SERIES FROM XBRL. This is the highest-value new series and it did not exist before FY2023.
  (a) Do NOT use the frames API for the production series. Frames match only exact calendar period ends, so filers with non-calendar fiscal years vanish: CY2025Q3I returns 11 filers versus 109 in CY2026Q1I, which is pure artefact. Use it only for a quick sanity check.
  (b) Production build: download the SEC Financial Statement Data Sets quarterly ZIPs (https://www.sec.gov/dera/data/financial-statement-data-sets.html), which contain sub.txt (one row per submission, with cik, adsh, period, fy, fp, form) and num.txt (every numeric XBRL fact, with adsh, tag, version, ddate, qtrs, value). Filter num.txt to tag IN ('SupplierFinanceProgramObligation','SupplierFinanceProgramObligationCurrent','SupplierFinanceProgramObligationNoncurrent') AND version LIKE 'us-gaap%' AND qtrs=0 (qtrs=0 identifies an instant/stock fact; qtrs>0 identifies a duration/flow). Join to sub.txt on adsh, keep form IN ('10-K','10-Q'), and take each cik's fact at its own ddate.
  (c) Deduplicate. Parent and subsidiary co-registrants both file: NextEra Energy and its Florida Power & Light co-registrant, Charter Communications and CCO Holdings, PennyMac Financial and PennyMac Mortgage Investment Trust. Map cik to an ultimate-parent group using the EDGAR company relationships or a hand-maintained list, and keep only the top of each group. My raw union of the five most recent frames gave 122 CIKs and $74.9bn; dedupe removes roughly $2-3bn.
  (d) For flows use tags SupplierFinanceProgramObligationIncrease and SupplierFinanceProgramObligationDecreaseSettlement with qtrs=4 for annual. Only ~26 of 122 filers tag the rollforward, so report flow coverage as a filer count alongside the dollar total and never present the flow total as complete.
  (e) Present the result as a percentage of the same filers' total accounts payable (us-gaap:AccountsPayableCurrent) so the reader sees the share of payables that has been converted into financial-creditor claims. That ratio, not the dollar level, is the risk metric.
  (f) Growth: CY2025Q4I 85 filers / $53.9bn versus CY2026Q1I 109 filers / $68.3bn. Because filer count moves with the frame, you MUST hold the filer panel constant to compute growth. Build a balanced panel of firms reporting in every period and report growth on that panel only.

STEP 3 — BUILD THE NON-BANK MORTGAGE STACK IN THREE LAYERS, because no single source spans it.
  Layer 1, ORIGINATION SHARE: HMDA LAR (https://ffiec.cfpb.gov/data-browser/), annual. Aggregate originations (action_taken=1) by lender type; non-bank = respondents with no depository charter. Cross-check against the FSOC benchmark of 64% of purchase originations in 2022. Note HMDA excludes business-purpose loans, so it does not see the RTL channel.
  Layer 2, SERVICING AND MSR: SEC XBRL for the public issuers. Pull us-gaap:ServicingAssetAtFairValueAmount and us-gaap:ServicingAsset (union both; some filers use both for different cohorts), then partition by SIC — 6162 is mortgage bankers and loan correspondents, 6798 captures the mortgage REITs (Rithm, Annaly, PennyMac Mortgage Investment Trust), 602x are the banks. At CY2026Q1I this gave 96 + 53 filers and $81.4bn + $37.8bn. Convert MSR carrying value to serviced UPB by dividing by the disclosed MSR multiple or by the capitalisation rate in each filer's servicing note — do NOT apply a market-wide multiple, the dispersion is large. For the private universe there is no route: the MBFRF is confidential and Inside Mortgage Finance is paid.
  Layer 3, FUNDING: FSOC's 2024 report is the only public source for warehouse and MSR line capacity ($285bn and $30bn of LIMITS at Q4 2023, ~55% uncommitted). For a current, higher-frequency proxy of DRAWN balances use Z.1 FL613168005. Do not equate the two: one is a committed-plus-uncommitted limit across a partly different universe, the other is a drawn balance for the Z.1 finance-company sector. For a forward series, the NMLS Mortgage Call Report Financial Condition component carries lines-of-credit capacity for state-licensed entities and is public; retrieve it from the NMLS statistical reports page manually — the site defeated scripted access here.

STEP 4 — CAPTIVE AND VENDOR FINANCE. Build the captive CIK list first, do not try to filter by tag. Use EDGAR company browse by SIC (https://www.sec.gov/cgi-bin/browse-edgar?action=getcompany&SIC=6141&type=10-K&count=100, and repeat for 6153, 6159, 6172), which returns the full historical universe including defunct filers; intersect with the submissions API to keep entities with a filing in the last 18 months. Then for each CIK pull us-gaap:NotesReceivableNet AND us-gaap:FinancingReceivableExcludingAccruedInterestBeforeAllowanceForCreditLoss and take whichever the filer uses. At CY2026Q1I: Ford Motor Credit $117.05bn, Toyota Motor Credit $101.00bn, GM Financial $87.86bn, Caterpillar Financial $32.64bn. For TECHNOLOGY vendor financing the captives are not separately registered, so go to the parent's 10-K: Dell Technologies $13.95bn (FYE 2026-05-01), HPE $9.21bn (2026-04-30), Cisco $6.58bn (2026-04-25) — read the "Financing Receivables" note in each, which splits customer loans from lease receivables and discloses the securitised/off-balance-sheet portion separately. Add the operating-lease and residual-value disclosures if you want the full customer-financing exposure. There is no way to capture vendor-ARRANGED third-party financing, where the vendor introduces a bank or leasing company and takes only a residual guarantee; that appears, if at all, in the guarantees and commitments note as narrative.

STEP 5 — TRADE CREDIT VELOCITY, NOT LEVEL. The level (NFC payables $4,523.2bn) is a stock and mostly rollover. Compute instead: (a) the flow, FA103170005, SAAR, four-quarter moving average to kill the Q1 artefact; (b) days payable outstanding for the Russell 3000 from XBRL (AccountsPayableCurrent / CostOfRevenue × 365), which is the cleanest evidence of terms extension; (c) the ratio of the STEP 2 supplier-finance obligation to accounts payable for the same firms. If DPO is rising AND the supplier-finance ratio is rising, terms extension is being financed rather than absorbed, and the buyer's effective leverage is understated by the difference.

STEP 6 — GLOBAL CROSS-CHECK. FSB GMR 2025 (https://www.fsb.org/uploads/P161225.pdf), Graph 1-6 table for the narrow measure and EF1–EF5, Graph 3-3 and 3-4 for EF2 by entity type and product, Box 2 Graph B2 for non-bank real estate exposure. EF2 at $6.1trn is the global analogue of Z.1 finance companies at $2.93trn — the US is roughly half. Do not add the two. Use the FSB series for direction and cross-jurisdiction comparison only; the twelve-month lag makes it useless for anything current.

STEP 7 — MARK THE HOLES EXPLICITLY IN THE OUTPUT. Every channel below should appear in the deliverable as a named row with "no published series" in the value column rather than being omitted, because omission reads as zero: CRE debt funds, residential transition lending, litigation finance, BNPL outstanding stock, drawn warehouse balances outside the Z.1 finance-company sector, MSR-backed notes outstanding, and global trade finance since 2011.

## Double-counting risk

Additive totals across this channel are wrong in at least eight distinct ways. The overlaps are structural, not incidental.

WITHIN THE Z.1 — the same loan appears in a sector table and an instrument table:
• Finance company mortgages appear as S125s2.1.s line 7 (FL613065000, $317.5bn) AND as F4.5.s line 44. Same dollars, two views. Same for ABS issuer mortgages ($1,136.6bn in S125s1.3.s line 7 and distributed across F4.5a/b/c.s), consumer credit, and business loans. Pick sector view OR instrument view, never both.
• mREIT securitised assets appear as a memo on the ABS issuer table (FL643065045, $173.0bn, S125s1.3.s line 20) explicitly flagged as already counted on the mREIT table S124.4.s. The Z.1 footnotes this; a naive column sum will double it.
• ABS issuer commercial paper of $198.2bn (line 21) is explicitly flagged as already included on the depository institution table S122.1.s line 47.

ASSET AND FUNDING OF THE SAME BALANCE SHEET:
• The drawn-warehouse series FL613168005 ($289.8bn) FUNDS the finance-company loan book. Adding warehouse borrowing to finance-company loans counts the same credit twice, once as an asset and once as its liability.
• MSR carrying values ($81.4bn + $37.8bn from XBRL) are the capitalised value of a fee stream on loans that are already counted in F4.5a.s as somebody's mortgage asset. MSRs are never additive to credit. They are a valuation of a servicing contract.

SUPPLIER FINANCE IS A SUBSET, NOT AN ADDITION:
• The $74.9bn of SEC-disclosed supplier finance program obligations sits INSIDE the $4,523.2bn of Z.1 nonfinancial corporate trade payables. That is the whole accounting point of the disclosure — the obligation stays classified as a payable. Adding them double-counts. The correct presentation is the ratio: SEC-registrant supplier finance obligations as a share of the same registrants' accounts payable.
• Separately, securitised trade receivables held by ABS issuers (FL673070003, $59.2bn) are the SUPPLIER side of trade receivables already counted in FL103070005. The receivable has been sold, not created.

CROSS-CHANNEL OVERLAPS WITH OTHER RESEARCHERS' TERRITORY — flag, do not sum:
• ABS issuer "miscellaneous assets (funding agreements)" of $291.0bn (FL673090543) is FABN — funding agreement backed notes issued against life insurer funding agreements. That is the insurance/FABN channel. Do not include it here.
• ABS issuer "other loans and advances" of $361.9bn (FL673069005) is footnoted as including loans backing domestically-issued CLO securities, and the Z.1 notes that loans backing US CLOs issued OUTSIDE the US sit in table S2.s line 15. That is the private-credit/CLO channel.
• CRE debt fund and private credit fund NAV facilities and subscription lines are bank credit to funds. That belongs to the fund-finance channel, and it is also the reason CRE debt funds cannot be called pure intermediation.
• Repo funding of mREITs and CRE CLO warehouses overlaps the repo/securities-financing channel.

CROSS-MEASUREMENT-BASE, THE WORST CASE:
• FSB EF2 ($6.1trn, year-end 2024, national submissions, jurisdiction-aggregated balance sheets) and Z.1 finance companies ($2.93trn, 2026:Q1, survey-benchmarked) measure overlapping entities on different bases, at different dates, with different consolidation rules — the FSB nets out finance companies prudentially consolidated into banking groups, the Z.1 does not. The US contribution to EF2 IS mostly the Z.1 finance-company sector. Summing them roughly doubles the US.
• BIS trade finance ($6.5-8trn, 2011, an annual FLOW of facilities provided) and Z.1 trade credit ($8.93trn, 2026:Q1, a STOCK of payables) are a flow and a stock fifteen years apart, measuring bank-intermediated facilitation versus inter-firm obligation. They are not comparable and certainly not additive, despite being numerically similar — which is exactly the kind of coincidence that produces a bad chart.
• BNPL GMV ($33.8bn, 2022) is an annual purchase FLOW. It is not a stock and must never appear in a column of outstandings.

THE ONE LEGITIMATE ADDITION: within a single Z.1 instrument table at a single date, the holder lines DO sum to the instrument total by construction — e.g. F4.5c.s lines 6 through 22 sum to $3,996.2bn. That is the only safe summation in this brief, and even there the trade credit table fails to reconcile by $889.3bn.

## What cannot be measured

Stated without hedging. These are real holes, and filling any of them with an estimate would be worse than leaving it empty.

1. CRE DEBT FUNDS HAVE NO SECTOR ANYWHERE IN THE US ACCOUNTS. I checked directly: Z.1 table F4.5c.s (commercial mortgages, 2026:Q1, $3,996.2bn) allocates every dollar to depositories, credit unions, life and P&C insurers, pension funds, GSEs, ABS issuers, finance companies, mREITs, governments and non-financial business. There is no debt-fund line. And S127.2.s "Other financial business", which people assume is the catch-all, is explicitly defined in its own footnote as funding subsidiaries, custodial accounts for reinvested securities-lending collateral, PPIP funds, Federal Reserve crisis SPVs and US central counterparties — total assets $1,529.3bn at 2026:Q1, of which loans are just $105.8bn. Debt-fund lending is absorbed into the household residual. You can watch it indirectly in FL153069803 (household-sector holdings of loans to nonfinancial corporate business), $131.0bn at 2026:Q1 against $85.3bn at end-2023, but that series is a plug and contains other things.

2. THE FSB SAYS THE SAME THING GLOBALLY AND SAYS IT OUT LOUD. Private credit funds are excluded from the narrow measure by construction, because they are closed-end, low-levered at the fund level, and do not use short-term wholesale funding — so they fall into neither EF1 nor EF2 nor any other economic function. Only eight jurisdictions were able to supply any private-credit disaggregation at all in the 2025 exercise (Canada, Germany, Italy, Luxembourg, Netherlands, Japan, Switzerland, Hong Kong). The United States, the largest market, is not among them.

3. THERE IS NO BNPL OUTSTANDING STOCK, AND THE FLOW IS FOUR YEARS OLD. The CFPB's $33.8bn of 2022 gross merchandise value is the last authoritative US number and it is a purchase flow, not a balance. The collection was a one-off market-monitoring order under 12 U.S.C. 5512(c)(4); it has not been repeated; the 2024 interpretive rule that would have brought BNPL into Regulation Z is no longer being enforced. G.19 excludes BNPL. Z.1 consumer credit is built from G.19 and therefore also excludes it. You could construct a stock from GMV and an assumed six-week tenor; the resulting number would be roughly $4bn and I decline to publish it, because the tenor assumption is doing all the work and 2022 volumes have no bearing on 2026.

4. RESIDENTIAL TRANSITION / FIX-AND-FLIP LENDING IS INVISIBLE BY DESIGN. These are business-purpose loans, so they are exempt from HMDA reporting. They are not consumer credit, so they are outside G.19. They are term-financed in 144A securitisations, so there is no Reg AB II asset-level ABS-EE filing. The only observable slice is rating-agency new-issue commentary on RTL deals, which is a paid, partial, issuance-only view of a market whose non-securitised portion is unknown.

5. LITIGATION FINANCE HAS NO AGGREGATE AT ALL. One listed pure-play files with the SEC (Burford). Everything else is private funds and captive insurer arrangements. No regulator collects it. Court-level disclosure rules are patchy and state-by-state and produce case documents, not a series. Anyone quoting an industry total is quoting a commercial survey of self-reported responses.

6. DRAWN WAREHOUSE CAPACITY IS NOT PUBLISHED. The FSOC's $285bn is a committed-plus-uncommitted LIMIT at Q4 2023 from a confidential form. Even the committed/uncommitted split (~55% uncommitted) rests on the subset of MBFRF respondents who broke it out, whose facilities totalled $186bn of the $285bn. The MBFRF itself — quarterly, 550+ filers, full financials and facility inventory — is not public in any form and has no release schedule. Z.1 FL613168005 gives drawn depository loans to the finance-company sector but that sector is not coterminous with the NMC universe.

7. THE ENTIRE US FINANCE-COMPANY SECTOR RESTS ON A FIVE-YEARLY SURVEY. Both G.19 and Z.1 benchmark finance companies to the FR 3033s Survey of Finance Companies, conducted every five years, and interpolate between benchmarks using the FR 2248 Domestic Finance Company Report. Between benchmarks you are looking at a smoothed extrapolation, and benchmark revisions are large. The FSB flags the same problem in its own footnotes: the 2021 jump in US EF2 is a structural break from re-benchmarking to that survey, and absent it US EF2 growth in 2021 would have been roughly flat.

8. TRADE CREDIT DOES NOT RECONCILE. Z.1 F81.s shows total payables of $8,933.6bn against total receivables of $9,822.8bn at 2026:Q1, a discrepancy of -$889.3bn, roughly 9% of the stock, and it has been persistently negative and widening (-$745.5bn in 2023, -$816.4bn in 2024, -$841.6bn in 2025). Both sides derive largely from the Census Quarterly Financial Report and IRS Statistics of Income, and SOI runs through 2023 only. The largest credit aggregate in this brief has a measurement error larger than most of the channels people write about.

9. GLOBAL TRADE FINANCE HAS NOT BEEN SIZED SINCE 2011. The BIS CGFS estimate of US$6.5–8trn is from January 2014 with 2011 data and has never been refreshed. BIS locational and consolidated banking statistics do not break out trade finance. The ICC Trade Register is member-restricted. SWIFT MT700 counts letters of credit, which is a shrinking and unrepresentative slice. Nothing published covers the reverse-factoring and platform-based supply-chain finance that grew up entirely after that report.

10. REVERSE FACTORING OUTSIDE US SEC REGISTRANTS IS UNMEASURED. ASU 2022-04 binds US GAAP filers. Private companies are out. Foreign private issuers filing 20-F under IFRS report under the 2023 IAS 7/IFRS 7 amendments, which mandate similar disclosure but are tagged in a different taxonomy and are not in the SEC us-gaap frames. And the disclosure is of the BUYER's obligation only — the funder-side exposure, and the identity and concentration of funders, is not disclosed. Greensill's failure was a funder-side concentration event that this disclosure regime would not have caught.

11. CONSUMER OPERATING LEASES ARE $219.8bn OF HOUSEHOLD OBLIGATION THAT IS NOT HOUSEHOLD DEBT. Z.1 FL613066303 carries it as a memo below the finance-company table precisely because, under the accounting, receivables from operating leases are recognised as income when payments are received and the leased vehicle is a non-financial asset of the lessor. It is real, contractual, monthly household outflow and it appears in no consumer-credit aggregate.

---
